# Constants and Conventions of the Wilson-Loop Notes

{#TP.1} Scope: the walk-factor note (W-δ, WILSON_IT1_WALK_FACTOR_DIFFERENCE_2026_10_28.md), the large-field note (LF, WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md), the Cauchy note (WILSON_IT1_CAUCHY_ESTIMATE_2026_10_27.md), the map and its closures, and the two-loops family (the two-loops, pair Cauchy, N-loops, and pair factorisation notes and the contacts draft). Every constant, exponent, threshold, domain, and convention that a note defines is entered in §§1–11, and every object a note defines for one argument is indexed in §12.

{#TP.1-1} Citations of the notes are tags, which do not move under edits: LF #n.m, Cauchy #Cs.n, W-δ #Ws.n, the map #Ms.n, the continuum note #Ns.n, BFWL #Bs.n, and the two-loops note #N, whose tags include those of its derivation files balaban_proofs/BP_TL_*.md (LABELS_TWO_LOOPS.md gives each tag's file). Each note's LABELS file maps its earlier line numbers to its tags. A source's page lines carry their page, p.N l.M.

{#TP.1-2} Every entry states the convention the notes use; a use that disagrees with this file is a defect of the note, not of the file. A row X too long for one line continues in the parts after it, tagged X-0-1, X-0-2, … and marked "continued"; a citation of X covers its continued parts, and a row tagged X-0 or X-0-k without that mark is an entry of its own. The note's own collected list of conditions is W-δ §8, *The conditions on the constants.* (#W8.12–#W8.23); 10.6 and §12 point to it. Absolute values and cardinalities are written abs(·) so that no cell contains a vertical bar.

## 1. Order of Choices {#TS1}

{#T1.1} Each constant is fixed in one stage and may depend only on constants of earlier stages. A condition is legal only if it is a lower bound on a quantity of its own stage in terms of earlier ones, or an upper bound on a quantity of a later stage.

| Stage | Fixed here | Kind of condition allowed |
|---|---|---|
| {#T1.2} 0 | d, G, and the exponents r, ε₀, β, β₂, β′, β″ | ranges: r := 2, CRE's exponent of (R_j ≥ (log g_j^{−2})^r) in (2.5) (#T2.23), which no paper fixes, not the r of rows other than #T2.23; r > 1 is what BP_TL_EVAL_POINTS #160 uses, and the printed conditions on it (LFR II p.383 l.28, 2p₁ − (d + 5)r₀ > p₀; p.363 l.40, ν ≥ 2p₀ + dr₀; LFR II's r₀ read as r, UV3 p.257's exponent of R, inferred, not printed, as BFWL #B2.2.294 reads it) are met by p₀, p₁, ν chosen after r; ε₀, β, β″, β₂, and β′ in the ranges of #T3.1–#T3.5; |
| {#T1.2-0-1} continued |  | CRE's β₀ := 1/7 of (2.6), "arbitrarily small, if g is sufficiently small" (CRE p.255 l.50), realised in stage 6 (covers β₀ ≤ 1/7, β₀ ≤ ½, and (1 + β₀)² ≤ 4/3 for LF's C_{αs} ≤ 2, LF #4.284.19; not the β₀ of other rows, RG I's Hölder β₀, or VAR's β₀ = 1); |
| {#T1.2-0-2} continued |  | CRE's q₁ = q₀ > p₀ + 3r, q₀ an integer, within the LF note's q₁ ≥ q₀ > p₀ + 3r (LF #4.420-1.1, #4.480; CRE (2.28)), 2q₀ > 3p₁ + p₀ + 7r + 1, which is q₀ > p₁ + ν_L/3 at #T3.41's ν_L = p₀ + q₀ + 7r + 1 and gives the two-loops note's q₀ > p₁ + θ_Lν_L for L ≥ 8 (BP_TL_EVAL_POINTS #86.3-2; #T3.40), and q₀ > 25r + 5/2 for NG's (O1c) (#NG8.3; NG work file #O1C.7-9.2), chosen after p₀, p₁, and r, before ν_L |
| {#T1.3} 1 | L | lower bounds in d, G, and stage 0's exponents: L ≥ 4, L > 5 (so L ≥ 6), L > 2d, L ≥ 8d + 4, L ≥ 12d + 6 (which covers L ≥ 8d + 4 and LF's L ≥ 8d + 6 of the class tail of ι_X, LF #4.323), L² > 9d, (1 + β₀)² ≤ L with CRE's β₀ = 1/7 (stage 0), given by L ≥ 6; L odd, L > 11, and L ≥ L₀ = 5000 (map #MD2.2.6.204, #MD2.4.6.16); L ≥ L₀^× := 4·10⁴u_𝔏, u_𝔏 = 640(2^{d+2} + 8), 1.8432·10⁹ at d = 4 (two-loops #288, #629-5), which covers L₀ and the map's L ≥ 486 (#MD2.4.10.5); L ≥ 6d + c_b (LF #4.541-1.1); L ≥ 128(1 + β₀), so that L₀ = 2 meets L₀² ≤ L/(32(1 + β₀)) (LF #4.643); (L-V) L^{5(1−ε₀)/128} ≥ 4 (LF #4.618) |
| {#T1.3-1} 1 | L (N loops) | L ≥ #T1.3's L₀^× for every N_C (N-loops #NL6A.9), and the 𝐑-made insertions, single-loop, cross, or merged, have #T1.3's u_𝔏 as units factor for every N_C (#NL6A.6, #NL6A.3-3), #NL6B.1-1's count using that a π_k-cube meets at most 8M bonds of ⊔_iC_i, all on one loop (#NL1.3-5). #NL12.1's conditions, with ν ≤ 1/1000 and c_π′α_{1,k+1}·4096M ≤ ν at every step (#NL6A.7), do not involve N_C; of the note's conditions only the scale conditions of #T12.33-4 do (#NL12.1-1) |
| {#T1.4} 2 | Bałaban's constants that depend on d, L, G (δ₀, δ₁, B₀–B₅, PBG's a₀ and a₁, VAR's a₃, O(1) of VAR, REG, PBG, PRT2, CRE) and the derived rates and constants (δ₀′, δ_c, δ_gf′, δ_D′, δ_ι, δ_min, δ_g, δ_br, δ_br^c, c_u, c_G, r_G, r_υ, c_d), the BH constants of the rows #T10.28-3-1–#T10.28-3-1-3-2, RG I's β_I ((1 + 8β_I)L^{−2} ≤ 1, RG I (3.52), p.280 l.18, which covers (1 + 7β_I)L^{−2} ≤ 1 of p.279 l.28), and stage 2's upper bounds on c₂ (c₂ ≤ 1, c₂ ≤ c_N, and the rooms of RG I's (3.13); W-δ #W8.15-1–#W8.15-2), c₂ itself fixed at stage 3′ (#T1.6) | none; computed |
| {#T1.5} 3 | R₀M₀, M′ (VAR's raised R₁M₁, #T2.7-1), K (PBG's and CRE's), M₁ = K_{M′}M′ (CRE's K, written K_{M′} in the LF), R, R_ι, RG II's M, λ_R, Bałaban's κ (#T3.34), before R (LF #4.582-2), RG II's κ₁ (#T10.56-2) after κ and before M (½(κ₁ − 1) ≥ 2Lκ, RG II p.20 l.17; δ₁M ≥ κ₁) | lower bounds in stages 0–2 and the sizes before it, in the order R₀M₀, then PBG's K (hence M′ = KR₀M₀; the lower bounds on M′ are met by K large), then CRE's K_{M′} and with it R (K_{M′} ≥ R_thr(d, L)/L + 1 with R_thr taken at the block size M′, BP_JOINS_BLOCK_SIZE #4.22-2-1), then RG II's M (W-δ §8, stage 3); |
| {#T1.5-0-1} continued |  | e.g. M′ ≥ LR₀M₀, M ≥ 4KM′, (D2) with C_ι(M′), and Bałaban's own lower bounds on M: RG I's M ≥ M(κ), RG II's δ₀M ≥ κ and δ₁M ≥ κ₁, CRE's p.245 l.34, LFR I's δ(M/M₁) ≥ 2 at p.186 l.35 and p.200 l.14–21; δ_ιM₁ ≥ 1 for §12's tail of ι_Y; (T-M₁) M₁ ≥ M₁^τ := (6/(eδ₀))(512B₃(1 + β₀)²C_ι/C_F)^{1/3} for the class tail of ι_X at every generation, LF #4.323-2.1, with C_ι/C_F = O(1)L²C_L^ι (LF #4.323-0.1; Cauchy #C9.8.15-3) independent of M₁ and L ≥ 8d + 6 from stage 1; M ≥ 2dM₁ for the LF note's strip response, LF #4.445 and #4.449; |
| {#T1.5-0-2} continued |  | M ≥ 2(d + 1)L²M₁ for the strip of misaligned layers, LF #4.445 and #4.449; (D-Λ), a lower bound on M/M₁ after M₁ and δ_V, LF #4.538–#4.539 |
| {#T1.5-1} 3 | M ((C-M) of SW) | (C-M) M ≥ 2LM₁ + 2R₁M₁ and M ∈ R₁M₁ℕ, with R₁M₁ #T2.7's printed product (R₁ = R₁^V, map #MD2.2.6.190-22, not CRE's scale R₁) and M₁ VAR's, not #T1.5's CRE M₁ (SW's opening, §5(1), §6(6e)) |
| {#T1.5-2} 3 | M (RW1), V4's M | V4's δ_M conditions of eq:c-standing-conditions at 4δ_M, which implies them: 4δ_M ≤ 1, h₊²(4δ_M)²/γ ≤ 1/8, and 4C_d(4δ_M)²(LM + 1)⁴ ≤ 1/2 (RW1 #RW1.5bp-2). They hold for M large, as δ_M = 2C_w^{V4}4^{−⌊M/(2R_*)⌋} decays exponentially in M while γ, h₊, C_d, n_*, R_*, and C_w^{V4} are fixed before M (V4 lem:gaussian-payment). RW1's τ_w (#T12.35-3) is fixed after M and before g₀ (#RW1.5bp-13) |
| {#T1.6} 3′ | VAR's a₀, a₁, a₅, and M(ε₁), taken at VAR's raised product R₁M₁ = M′ (#T2.7-1): a₅ depends on R₁M₁ through (166) (VAR p.304 l.21–26) and a₁ through a₅ (l.52–54), so here both depend on M′, not on d and L only as at VAR's printed R₁M₁ (p.279 l.28); VAR prints no dependence of a₀ on R₁M₁ (p.280 l.42, p.294 l.47), and B₃ has none ((162), p.303 l.41); B₃⁺ (Cauchy #C5.37-18) and c₂, under stage 2's upper bounds and c₂ ≤ c_* (W-δ #W8.20–#W8.20-1), stages 1–3 using c₂ only through c₂ ≤ 1 (#W8.20-2) | c₂: upper bounds in stages 0–3 and B₃⁺; the rest none, computed after stage 3 |
| {#T1.7} 4 | PBG's α₀ (with #T3.15's α_J), then PBG's α₁ (RG I's α's are not here; #T3.13) | upper bounds in stages 0–3′ (§8, stage 4); never in terms of ε₁ |
| {#T1.8} 5 | VAR's ε₃ first, then ε₁ (VAR's, and 10.5's fixed value), ε₁^{(1)} (O(1)C_ℒ^gB₅ε₁ ≤ ε₃/2 bounds ε₁ by ε₃) | upper bounds in stages 0–4 (§8, stage 5) |
| {#T1.9} 6 | γ (hence ḡ, ε_k, the data parameters, ε_U^{(i)}, ϱ, ϱ̄, α_c, d̄(ω), ε₂, α₀^W, ε₄) | γ small (§8, stage 6), including g small enough for CRE (2.6) at β₀ = 1/7, and α small after d, κ, β, and ν (two-loops #814-4-3-2) |

{#T1.10} Rule 1: no condition bounds α₀ or α₁ above by ε₁, by ε_k, or by ϱ.
{#T1.11} Rule 2: VAR's upper bound M_V ≤ M(ε₁) = R₁M₁a₁/ε₁ is used as an upper bound on ε₁ (stage 5), never as an upper bound on a stage-3 size.
{#T1.12} Rule 3: an O(1) belongs to the stage of the result it comes from; an O(1) that depends on L is written with its L-power whenever a condition compares two L-powers (c_u = O(1)L³, the L⁵ at the index j + 1, the L⁴ of the axial-gauge input, the L⁷ of the gradient at #W10.5.47).
{#T1.13} Rule 4: a constant that may grow with a size (C₀ and C_F carry R₁M₁ = M′; C_ι = O(1)L²C_LC_F) never enters a lower bound on that size alone; either the condition is an upper bound on ε₁ (for example LC_ιε₁ ≤ O(1)M₁α₀), or the growing constants enter as a ratio bounded independently of the size (C_ι/C₀ ≤ O(1)L⁴C_LB₃ at Cauchy #C9.8.14-3-1, from VAR (8), whose space 𝔘_k(B₃ε₁) carries no cube factor; VAR (9)'s B₃M does, and is not used for the ratio).
{#T1.14} Rule 5: every condition the note uses on its constants is entered in W-δ §8's list when it is written, with its source; a condition stated only where it is derived is a defect. Bałaban's own conditions that the notes rely on (lower bounds on M, the RG I and RG II smallness conditions) are entered as well.

## 5. Conditions {#TS5}

{#T5.1} The full list, by stage and with its sources, is W-δ §8, *The conditions on the constants.* (#W8.12–#W8.23). It is not copied here, so that it exists once. When a derivation adds or changes a condition, the condition is entered there in its stage, and the derivation states it where it arises (rule 5).

## 6. Conventions {#TS6}

- {#T6.1} Distances: ℓ∞ unless stated; an ℓ¹ separation s gives an ℓ∞ separation ≥ s/d, and the factor d is absorbed by R ≥ 12dL.
- {#T6.2} Layers: run A's layer j is run B's layer j + 1; a_{j(x)} is the scale of the global layer of x; on run A's shell n, a_{j(x)} ≥ L^nε except on the innermost local shell (factors L and L²).
- {#T6.3} Units: F and its first differences carry ε^{−2} times the dimensionless differences of the link variables; B′ and ε₂(ε/a_j)² are dimensionless.
- {#T6.4} Cubes: for RG I's □ ∈ π_j, of side M, □̃^k is the concentric cube of side (1 + 2k)M (RG I p.257 l.3–7), so □₀ = □̃⁵ has side 11M (Cauchy #C5.4); for PBG's □ ∈ 𝒟_j, a union of 2^d big blocks of side M′, □̃^k is the concentric cube of side (2 + 2k)M′, so □̃⁵ has side 12M′ (PBG p.408 l.20–24); VAR's class cube of parameter M_V has side 2M_Va_j, is a union of big blocks (aligned to the grid), and its test cube adds 2R₁M₁a_j on each side (side (2M_V + 4R₁M₁)a_j). Aligning a cube to the grid costs at most one block per side.
- {#T6.5} CMP98 (15): the block average is an average over the L^d contours Γ_{c,x} of the logarithms of the closed-contour factors; each contour has at most L + d(L − 1) ≤ (d + 1)L fine bonds. In LFR I (1.36) and LFR II (1.61)–(1.63), exp iQ̃_·(A) = M̃^·(exp iA) is CMP98's relative average Ũ of (65), (68)–(69), the average of U′U₀ times that of U₀ inverted (LFR II p.366 l.28, LFR I (1.35)).
- {#T6.5-0-1} CMP98 (15), continued. The composed average of (89)–(91) is (R_{c₋}-average)^{−1}ŨR̄₀(R_{c₊}-average), so it equals u_−^{−1}e^{iQ̃}R(u_+). The gauge factor u_k^0 = u(𝐇) is CMP98 (106) (p.33 l.43–46), fixed by (77) and (81) as a function of the field alone (p.30). So M^·((e^{iA}U)^{u(A)^{−1}}) = u(A)_−^{−1}e^{iQ̃(A)}R(u(A)_+)M^·(U) holds for every field A near 0, at a fixed background U, by (88)–(92), at the decoupled 𝐇(s) of LFR II p.373 l.31–34 included ((1.61) prints U_1, equal to U_{1,2} on Ω″^∼_{h+1} by (1.53)), and the jumps of u at sub-block faces are gauge.
- {#T6.5-1} CMP98 (92), (97), (99) (p.031 l.55–59, p.032 l.28–35, l.49–53): for U = U₁U₀, the relative k-th average is (Ũ₁^k)_b = v_k(b₋)(Ū̿₁^k)_bR̄^k_{0,b}v_k^{−1}(b₊) = (Ū̿₁^k)_b^{v_k}, with R̄_{0,c} := R((V̄₀)_c) the conjugation (p.027 l.35; #T11.19-1's R(U)), Ū̿₁^k the composed average of (89)–(91), and v_k(x) = (R_{0,x}U₁)‾(R̄_{0,x}Ū̿₁)‾⋯(R̄^{k−1}_{0,x}Ū̿₁^{k−1})‾ (97), which is the k-th average of R_{0,x}U₁ at x (99). So (1/i)log Ũ₁^k and Q_k := (1/i)log Ū̿₁^k differ in general by the factors v_k(b±).
- {#T6.5-2} CMP98 Proposition 3 (p.036 l.49–52): there are C₁ = C(d) and c₃ = c(d, L) ≤ c₂ such that, for α₀, α₁ ≤ c₃, Q(V₀, A) = (1/i)log V̄̿₁ is analytic in A with (122)–(124); so it holds at 𝔤^c-valued A in that ball. By (127) (p.037 l.2–8), (1/i)log Ū̿₁^k is the composition Q_k(U₀, ηA) of Q(Ū₀^j, ·), j < k, and for U₀ under Proposition 2's assumptions and U₁ = e^{iηA}, abs(A) < α₁, (130)–(131) (p.038 l.1–15) give abs(Q_j(U₀, ηA)) < 2α₁L^jη; for α₁ ≤ ½c₃ Proposition 3 then applies at each step (p.038 l.17). For the chain's dressed average: Cauchy #C10.1.4-1-1-1.
- {#T6.6} Citations: transcription line numbers count the \pagemark line as l.1; in *_corrected.tex, file line = print line. REG (CMP 99) and UV3 have no *_corrected files, and their pages open with a "%" line before the \pagemark, so a REG or UV3 pin is the file line minus 1 (REG p.077 l.31 for "the following spaces of regular field configurations", file line 32).
- {#T6.6-0-1} Citations, continued. Bałaban by page and line (verification/BALABAN_LINE_PINS.md). Quotations of Bałaban's smallness are his words (PBG p.396 l.32: "O(1)Mα₀ is still a sufficiently small number"), not glosses such as "absolute threshold". PBG: the series quote at p.422 l.12–13, the (QGQ*)^{−1} remark at p.422 l.15, (3.132) at p.422 l.17–21, the Δ^{(2)} bound at p.422 l.50–52.

- {#T6.10} (RZ): in LFR I §1 and LFR II (1.50)–(1.55) the regions Z_j stand for their intersections with Z, the union of the class of components ("For simplicity we denote intersections of the regions Z_j with Z by Z_j also … We write the intersections explicitly only if it may lead to a misunderstanding", LFR I p.177 l.33–35), so (Ω″_{h+1})^c, (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c, and (Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c are complements within Z (LF #4.172).
- {#T6.10-1} Each component of Z, of either class of LFR II p.378 l.14–15, lies in a cube of side 100MR_k. Z is the union of a class of components of the large field region Λ_k^c, each "contained in a cube of the size 100MR_k" (LFR I p.177 l.17, l.26, l.32–33), the size being a side, since ten layers of MR_k-cubes give "greater than 20MR_k" (l.18–19). LFR II continues LFR I's operation R (LFR II p.355 l.11) and uses the bound at scale j + 1 (p.386 l.3).
- {#T6.10-1-0-1} Components in 100MR_k-cubes, continued. Used by LF #4.57-3 and #4.291-2, two-loops #613, #614, #654, #804, and #814-4-2-4, BP_TL_EVAL_POINTS #78-4 and #79, BLL #BLL.1-1, #BLL.4, and #BLL.36-2, D2.7 §7, 15.5(i-5-1)–(i-5-2), 15.5(ii)(β-4)–(β-5-0), 15.7, and 15.8, the general-loops note's #GL5.2-7, and the N-loops note's #NL6B.1-2.
- {#T6.11} The current variable 𝐉 replaces the functions J_j = D^{ξ*}_{U_j}ξ^{−2}π Im∂U_j, the only ones in which the second-order derivative appears, which appear in the expansions of the action and in the propagators (RG I (1.8)–(1.9), p.261 l.34–48). In LFR II (1.61)–(1.65) the averages of configurations become M^·(𝐔) (#T11.7-0-2) and V_Λ^{(A)}(M^k(𝐔)), and 𝐉 enters only through 𝐇″_k, 𝐇_{𝐁₁}, and 𝐇_k (p.374 l.37–40, p.375 l.5–11; LF #4.288-0.1, #4.410-2).
- {#T6.11-1} CRE's 𝐉_{p,X}(M^·(𝐔)) of (2.37) is RG I's J_n of (1.15) at n = p on X: the current (1.8), D^{ξ*}_{U}ξ^{−2}π Im∂U, at U = U_{p,X}(M^·(𝐔)) := U(𝐁_p(X) ∪ {Γ_i}_{i<p}, M^·(𝐔)) and ξ = L^{−p}. CRE prints no formula; its spaces are defined "similarly as in (I.1.11)–(I.1.16)" (p.260 l.54–55, p.261 l.2, l.10), and RG I defines J_n "by the formula (1.8) with U_n(M^·(𝐔)) instead of U_j, L^{−n} instead of ξ" (p.262 l.49–50). CRE (2.37)'s room α_{0,n}L^{n−min{p,n}}(L^nL^{−p})^{−3} (p.261 l.21) is (2.36)'s at ξ = L^{−p}, times L^{n−min{p,n}}.

𝒮_z is the subclass at which this holds on □₀ (Cauchy #C5.13).

It equals the (3.11) current exactly when D_𝐔(RD_𝐔*𝐀) = 0.

On Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} it is CRE p.277's assertion "we have to use a part of the analyticity domains of terms in 𝐁_k for the function 𝐇_k" (Bałaban's, accepted; map #MD2.2.4.74–78); on Ω_{k+1} it is derived from CRE (2.4) and (2.28) under #T6.17 for g_K small (map #MOMGD.49). Its radius and domain are #T3.36's r_{α,k} and 𝒫_k^m.

CRE prints X^{∼−1} = ((X^c)^∼)^c (p.247 l.49). Touching is having a common point, forced by CRE (#T6.19-3), so X^∼ adds every cube of X's family that meets X.
- {#T6.19-1} Compatible partitions (CRE p.245 l.38–40, p.257 l.43) are nested, every cube of the coarser partition a union of cubes of the finer, the sense of REG p.98 l.19–20: "i.e. for every j the cube □_j is a sum of the big blocks of the lattice T_{L^{−j}}", which CRE forces (#T6.19-4).
- {#T6.19-2} By the nesting of #T6.19-1 and #T6.19's touching, □^∼ is the cube of side (L + 2)M₂R₀ centred at □.
- {#T6.19-3} CRE (1.10) and p.248 l.8–9 force #T6.19's touching. Let Q₁ be CRE's large-field region of (1.8) (p.247 l.42–50) and Q₁′ the union of the LMR₁-cubes meeting Q₁ (p.248 l.2). Were cubes meeting only at a vertex not touching, an LMR₁-cube at offset (2,2,2,1) from Q₁′ could lie in Ω₁ within 2LMR₁ of Q₁ (V4 sec:sourced-effective-interaction; IT-1 §3c(d)).
- {#T6.19-4} CRE forces #T6.19-1's nesting: it introduces the LMR₁- and L²M₂R₁-partitions by their sides only (p.246 l.23–27), subject to the compatibility of p.245 l.38–40, and the functions (1.19) remain only on the L²M₂R₁-cubes in Ω₁∖Ω₁^{∼−1} or in the region of (1.18), a union of such cubes in Ω₁^{∼−1} (p.250 l.36–37, l.49–50), so that, for an admissible Ω₁ chosen at each face, no L²M₂R₁-cube crosses a face of an LMR₁-cube (V4 sec:main-result (H4); IT-1 §3c(d)).
- {#T6.20} χ_Ax: the axial-gauge characteristic function. It arises from the identity (1.5) (CRE p.247 l.2–6), the product over y ∈ P₁¹ and x ∈ B(y), x ≠ y, of ∫du(x)(1/z)χ({abs(U^u(y,x) − 1) < ε₀})exp[−g₀^{−2}(1 − Re tr U^u(y,x))], inserted under the integral with the Faddeev–Popov procedure, which gives χ_{Ax}(P₁¹) in (1.6) (p.247 l.12); at step k, χ_{Ax}((Ω_{k+1}^∼)^{(k)}) = 1 by the axial gauge for V_{□′}^{(k)} and A₁/A₀ small (CRE p.266 l.33–42).

The δ-function of each c ∈ Ω₁^{(1)} is σ₀^{−1}δ_𝔤 of the constraint function Q̃(A′, c) (UV3 (14)–(16)); A′ = A − hD̃(A) turns it into LQ̃A (RG1 p.267 l.26; V4 eq:native-source-local-implicit); and the equality (1.14) has no constant beyond abs(Ω₁*) log σ₀ (#T6.20-2), (L⁴ − 1)abs(Ω₁^{(1)}) log z with #T6.20's z, and E, the normalisation of CRE's initial density ϱ₀ := exp[−(1/g₀²)A − E], A the Wilson action (CRE p.243 l.29–30), whereas δ(LQ̃A) = L^{−dim 𝔤·abs(Ω₁^{(1)})}δ(Q̃A) for RG1's operator Q̃.
- {#T6.20-2} CRE's σ in (1.14)–(1.15) is σ_H/σ_H(0) bondwise (#T10.36-4). The Haar density σ₀(σ/σ₀)(A′(b)) of each bond of Ω₁ (UV3 p.260 l.20–21) and the σ₀^{−1} of each c ∈ Ω₁^{(1)} (UV3 (16)) leave σ₀^{abs(Ω₁)−abs(Ω₁^{(1)})} = σ₀^{abs(Ω₁*)} (CRE p.249 l.16–17), the factor (1.14) displays, so the equality (1.14) leaves σ/σ₀ in the integrand, as UV3 (18) prints it (p.260 l.30–31) in the transformation that CRE p.248 l.52–53 cites.

- {#T6.23} Norms: abs(·) in the LF and the Cauchy note is CMP98 (19)'s operator norm abs(X) = sup abs(Xψ) over unit ψ ∈ ℂ^N (p.021 l.8–16), in the fixed faithful unitary representation G ⊂ U(N), on G, 𝔤, 𝔤^c, and matrices; it is Ad(G)-invariant, conjugation by a unitary preserving it. ‖X‖² := tr X*X with tr X := N^{−1}Σ_jX_{jj}, the normalised trace of CMP98 (17) (p.020 l.34), and (20) gives abs(tr X) ≤ abs(X), ‖X‖ ≤ abs(X) ≤ √N‖X‖, abs(XY) ≤ abs(X)abs(Y) (p.021 l.17–24).

## 7. Implications Between Conditions {#TS7}

- {#T7.1} R ≥ 12dL implies R ≥ 8dL, R ≥ (2d + 1)L, and, for the walk problems, VAR's Sect. F condition in the form R′ > 4dL (#T2.20; for the lift, #T2.22's R_ιM₁/M₁^{VAR} > 4dLR₁); at the joins it is K ≥ 12d + 1, which implies the reach's K ≥ 10d + 1 and the lift's K ≥ 4d + 1.
- {#T7.2} L ≥ 12d + 6 implies L ≥ 8d + 4; with M₁ ≥ 4 it gives M₁(L/3 − 1 − R_ι/L) > 3 (#W12.58) and M₁(L − 1 − R_ι/L) > 3 (#W10.5.24).
- {#T7.3} M ≥ 4M₁ with CRE's M₁ = KM′ implies VAR's M ≥ 4R₁M₁, since R₁M₁ = M′ ≤ KM′.
- {#T7.4} ε₁ ≤ a₁/7 makes M_V = 7M′ admissible (M(ε₁) = M′a₁/ε₁ ≥ 7M′); ε₁ ≤ a₁/(3L) makes M_V = 3LR₁M₁ admissible and, for L ≥ 3, implies ε₁ ≤ a₁/7 wherever the same ε₁ is meant.
- {#T7.5} O(1)L⁴C₀ε₁ ≤ α₀ implies the current bounds O(1)Mα₀a^{−3} wherever PBG prints them, and, at α₀ = ε₀, VAR's B₃ε₁ ≤ ε₀ since C₀ ≥ 4R₁M₁B₃ ≥ B₃.
- {#T7.6} The conditions of 10.3 and 10.4 on R₀M₀ imply those of the compositions (Comp).
- {#T7.7} L > 5 with R_{h+1} ≥ 1 gives 4LR_{h+1} ≥ 24; with R_{h+1} ≥ L (#T2.23, for CRE's R_k and for the tube's R^T_k) it gives 4LR_{h+1} ≥ 144.

## 9. Checks Before Each Packet {#TS9}

- {#T9.1} Grep for the canonical strings of Sections 2–4 and for their known stale variants: "4R₁M₁" (only VAR's own M ≥ 4R₁M₁ at #W12.142 and its implication in §8), "M_V ≥ 6M₁", "a₁/15", "R ≥ 8dL" (allowed only as covered by 12dL), "K ≥ 10d + 1" (only the reach), "α₁^A" in the B″ chain, "O(1)Mα₀" through (3.35), "B₄(1)M_V", "absolute threshold", "lower bound on M" next to C_ι, "R/L" without R_ι.
- {#T9.1-0-1} Grep checks, continued. Also pointers to lists other than §8's ("listed with §12", "listed at the end of §12", "listed in 10.6", "collected in 10.6", "as in 10.6", "listed among §12's conditions", "§12's own list"), "leaves free" for an exponent §8 fixes, "uniformly in M′" next to VAR (9), "B₅ε₁ ≤ a₁" or "B₅ε₁^{(1)} ≤ a₁" without the halving, "min(α₂", "α₂^{RG I}", "R₁M₁ for RM" and "RM = R₁M₁" at the composites (M′), "c₀(¼)^d" in a count at α = ¼.
- {#T9.1-0-2} Grep checks, continued. "VAR (10)" or "VAR (9)" next to ∂□₀ or ∂Y_i (the class (8) there), "5−β)" for the irrelevant terms (β_E), "C″d̄" at the near domains at ∂Y_i (C″_∂), and "for M large" (RG I's α₂ and α₃ are the Cauchy note's, and the lower bound on M is (D2)), and "≪" in place of a stated condition.
- {#T9.2} Sweep the note for conditions not in §8's list: grep "under ", "log L", "lower bound", "upper bound", "for γ small", "for α₀ small", "≤ ½", "≤ 1", "≪", "halving", and "sufficiently small" outside #W8.12–#W8.23 and check each hit against the list (rule 5); conditions named collectively in the list ("the conditions on ϱ̄ of §11") are spelled out there one by one.
- {#T9.3} For each reason given for a bound, check that the cited display states it (a source that bounds a constant is quoted with its page and line, not described).
- {#T9.4} For each symbol taken from a cited paper (α₀, α₁, α₂, ε₁, C₀, C₁, M, K), check which paper's object it is at that place and whether it is fixed or γ-dependent; a relation between γ-dependent thresholds is homogeneous and is not entered as an upper bound of stage 4. Any suspected clash is settled against the source before launch, never left for the referee.
- {#T9.5} Run every check of this section on the final hash immediately before the packet is built.
- {#T9.6} For each new or changed inequality, record its stage and kind (Section 1), check rules 1–5, and enter it in W-δ §8's list.
- {#T9.7} For each new constant, add a row here before using it in a note; for each new use of M, M₁, K, ε₁, ε₀, a₀, a₁, C_ι, R, or c^A, check its meaning against the rows above.
- {#T9.8} For each new cube fit, add a row to Section 8 with the side, the reach, and the margin.
