## 11. Source Definitions {#TS11}

{#T11.1} The definitions the notes rely on, quoted from the parts files, so that they are read here and not re-derived from the sources.

- {#T11.2} PRT2's bond sets (PRT2 p.224 l.17–19; Lemma 2.4, p.245 l.75–76; (2.97), p.240; (2.120)–(2.122), p.244). For a set of sites Ω, "Ω" also denotes the set of bonds st(Ω) = ∪_{x∈Ω}st(x), the bonds with at least one endpoint in Ω (p.224), and Lemma 2.4 applies this to Λ = B(Λ′).
- {#T11.2-0-1} PRT2's bond sets, continued. The mass a Σ_{b∈Λ^c}abs((Q_jA)(b))² of (2.97) and ‖B↾_{Λ^c}‖² of (2.120) and (2.122) run over the bonds with both endpoints in Λ^c, the bonds outside Λ of Lemma 2.4 (p.245 l.76–77) (inferred, not printed): on a bond ⟨x, x′⟩ with x ∈ Λ, x′ ∈ Λ^c, invariance under λ = H_j′μ would force μ(x) = μ(x′) = 0 by (2.103), beyond (2.104), μ = 0 on Λ^c, which "matches our needs exactly" for (2.97)'s ω, integrated on Λ subject only to Q₁′ω = 0 (p.240 l.26; p.241 l.27–33).
- {#T11.2-0-2} PRT2's bond sets, continued. So the crossing bonds lie in Λ and are free, as LF #0.1.15 reads them and #0.1.16-0.2, #0.1.18, and #0.1.20's 1_m use. A mass bond joins adjacent sites, of one block or of two blocks sharing a codimension-1 face, so Λ^c blocks meeting only along a stratum of codimension ≥ 2 share no mass bond (LF #0.1.18-4).
- {#T11.3} PRT1's Δ_k and σ_k (PRT1 (1.62), p.28 l.24–29; (1.65)–(1.67), p.29 l.3–30). H_kB is the minimum of ½⟨∂A, ∂A⟩ on {A : Q_kA = B, R∂*A = 0} (p.29 l.4–5), and ⟨B, Δ_kB⟩ = ⟨∂H_kB, ∂H_kB⟩ (1.65).
- {#T11.3-0-1} PRT1's Δ_k and σ_k, continued. By (1.66), ⟨B, Δ_kB⟩ = ⟨∂₁B, σ_k∂₁B⟩, with σ_k = φ_μ^{−1}φ_ν^{−1}(∂₁*φ^{−1}∂₁)^{−1} on the (μν) plaquette component, of momentum symbol [(Σ_λabs(∂¹_λ(p′))²/(Δ₀²(p′)φ_λ(p′)))Δ₀(p′)φ_μ(p′)Δ₀(p′)φ_ν(p′)]^{−1}, φ_μ(p′) = Σ_l abs(u(p′ + l))²abs(v_μ(p′ + l))²/Δ(p′ + l) (1.62). The symbol "is bounded from below and above by positive constants γ₀, γ₁ dependent on d only" (p.29 l.27), so γ₀ ≤ σ_k ≤ γ₁ on every 2-form, not only on the exact ones, and (1.67) follows.
- {#T11.3-0-2} PRT1's Δ_k and σ_k, continued. Δ_k depends on ∂₁B only, so Δ_k∂ = 0. PRT2 (2.118) (p.243) is the same bound for Δ_j, "given by (1.66) and satisfying (1.67)". Decay: PRT2 p.250 l.21 ("C is a short-ranged operator, so C*Δ_kC has the same exponential decay as Δ_k") states an exponential decay of Δ_k, and PBG p.428 l.29 one of C*Δ_kC; no decay of σ_k itself is printed on these pages. LF #0.1.17-1-1 uses the exponential decay at the unit scale of Δ_j's kernel, which PRT2 p.250 l.21 states, and none of σ_k.
- {#T11.4} CRE (1.17) (CRE p.250 l.1–15). The expressions (1.14)–(1.16), except the characteristic function χ′₀(Ω₁), are invariant under the simultaneous transformations U₁ → U₁^u, A → R(u)A, (R(u)A)(x, x′) = u(x)A(x, x′)u^{−1}(x), "where u is a G-valued function defined on sites of the lattice T" (p.250 l.15). The invariance is printed for G-valued u only; the G^c-valued version used for the analytic extensions is RG I (1.10) (RG I p.262 l.4–7).
- {#T11.5} Lawler–Limic, Random Walk: A Modern Introduction (author draft, D:\Downloads\srwbook.pdf; pages of the draft).
- {#T11.5-0-1} Lawler–Limic, continued. 𝒫_d (§1.1, p.10): the finite-range, symmetric, irreducible walks given by a generating set V and κ : V → (0, 1] with Σκ ≤ 1; simple random walk is V = {e₁, …, e_d}, κ = 1/d. Its §6.5, capacity and Propositions 6.5.1, 6.5.2, and 6.5.5, is #T11.23.
- {#T11.6} CMP98's normalisation u (CMP98 (85)–(87), p.31 l.1–18). (85) defines the averages \overline{R_{0,x_{j+1}}U₁}^{(j+1)} recursively, as the average over x_j ∈ B(x_{j+1}) of the products of R̄^j_{0,x_{j+1}}Ũ₁^j along the contours Γ_{x_{j+1},x_j} with the previous averages at x_j. (84) and (81) give (86), u(y)\overline{R_{0,y}U₁}^{(k)} = 1 for y ∈ Ω^{(k)}, hence (87), u(y) = (\overline{R_{0,y}U₁}^{(k)})^{−1}.
- {#T11.6-1} CMP98 Proposition 6 (p.043 l.3–10). If U₀ satisfies (52), abs(U(∂p) − 1) < α₀η², η = L^{−k} (p.026 l.12–15), then \overline{U′U₀}^k is analytic in the 𝔤^c-valued A′ = (1/iη)log U′ for abs(A′) < α₁, with abs(\overline{U′U₀}^k(Ū₀^k)^{−1} − 1) < O(1)α₁ (164), assuming "that α₀, α₁ are sufficiently small" (l.10); the neighbourhood "may be also described by" abs(UU₀^{−1} − 1) < α₁η (158) (p.042 l.16–21). Used at LF #4.561.1-0.0-1; #T12.9-2's α₀^{CMP} is its α₀.
- {#T11.7-0} RG I's lattice sets (RG I p.251 l.26–28, p.252 l.8–12). A union of cubes of a division determines its lattice points of a given scale, its bonds b, the intervals joining nearest-neighbour points b₋, b₊ that meet it, and its plaquettes, the elementary squares that meet it, all denoted by the set's symbol (p.251 l.26–28); U(∂p) = (∂U)(p) is the plaquette variable, tr the normalised trace (p.252 l.8–12) (#BC.1-1-1).
- {#T11.7-0-1} RG I's blocks and contours ((0.3), p.252 l.21–32). For y in a lattice T_δ^{(n)}, B^k(y) := Δ(y) ∩ T^{(n−k)}_{L^{−k}δ}, Δ(y) the continuous cube of size δ centred at y, and B(y) := B¹(y). For x ∈ B(y), x − y = Σ_μδn_μe_μ, 𝐆(y, x) is the family of shortest contours from y to x, one per permutation π of the axes with n_μ ≠ 0, running abs(n_{π(1)}) bonds along sign(n_{π(1)})e_{π(1)} from y, then abs(n_{π(2)}) along the next axis, and so on (#BC.1-1-1).
- {#T11.7-0-2} RG I's averages M^·(𝐔) (RG I (1.15), p.262 l.49): M^·(𝐔, b) = M^p(𝐔, b) = Ū^p(b) for b ∈ Λ_p, Ū^p the p-th average of 𝐔 (RG I's (1.5) [14]), so M^p(𝐔) is its level-p part; LFR II (1.61)–(1.65) use it as in #T6.11 (#BC.6-1).

- {#T11.7} RG I's U^c_j(X, α₀, α₁, γ₀) (RG I p.262 l.12–55), a union of G^c-orbits of pairs on X with four conditions. (i) 𝐔 = U′U, U G-valued, abs(∂U − 1) < α₀ξ² on X, and a G-gauge per O(1)LM-cube with abs(A), abs(∇A) < O(1)LMBα₀ (1.12). (ii) U′ = exp iξA′, abs(A′), abs(∇_UA′) < α₁ on X. (iii) abs(∂𝐔 − 1) < α₀ξ² and abs(𝐉) < γ₀ on X. (iv) X taken as Ω₀ with its maximal sequence {Ω_n} by (1.3)–(1.6) [14]; the pair (U_n(M^·(𝐔)), J_n(M^·(𝐔))) satisfies (1.16), abs(∂U_n − 1) < α₀ξ² and abs(J_n) < α₀(L^nξ)² on X̃^{−2}, and the same holds for U. (iv) is imposed at the α₀ of (i).
- {#T11.8} RG I (1.17) (p.263 l.7–22). (i)–(iii) at α₀′ give abs(∂U_n − 1) < 2B₃α₀′ξ² and abs(J_n) < 2B₃α₀′(L^nξ)² by Proposition 9 [15], so (iv) holds once 2B₃α₀′ ≤ α₀. It is printed for every pair satisfying (i)–(iii) at α₀′, complex ones included (p.263 l.7–19), the real minimal configurations being the special case of p.263 l.19–22.
- {#T11.9} Lemma 4's input space U′^c_{k+1}(□₀, (1+2β)α₀, (1+2β)α₁, α₀) (RG I p.275 l.19–21; p.277 l.34, p.278 l.26; written without its fourth argument at p.280 l.25).
- {#T11.9-0-1} Lemma 4's input space, continued. It has the conditions (i)–(iv), with □₀ as Ω₀ in (iv) and (i)–(iii) "on the whole lattice T_η". Only □₀ is used (the Cauchy note's #C5.21). The proof uses (iv) only through (3.40) (p.278 l.26–34), abs(∂U_{k+1}(□₀, M^·(𝐔)) − 1) < (1+2β)α₀(L^{−1}η)² and abs(J_{k+1}(□₀, M^·(𝐔))) < (1+2β)α₀ on □̃³, which feed (3.41)–(3.42) through (3.39); "(iv)" occurs in pp.275–280 otherwise only at p.275 l.20 and for the output (p.278 l.2, "all the conditions (i)–(iv)"; p.278 l.13; p.279 l.49; p.280 l.21), whose (iv) comes from (iii) by (3.38) (p.278 l.12–13).
- {#T11.9-0-2} Lemma 4's input space, continued. (3.26)–(3.27) (p.275 l.21–38), from (i)–(iii): M^·(𝐔) = V^v, abs(V(b) − 1) < O(1)Mα₀, abs(v − 1) < O(1)Mα₁; U_{k+1}(□₀, V) = (exp iL^{−1}η𝐇_{k+1})^{u_{k+1}}, abs(𝐇_{k+1}), abs(∇𝐇_{k+1}) < B₃O(1)Mα₀ on □̃⁴, abs(u_{k+1} − 1) < B₃O(1)Mα₀ on □₀. Covariance: U_n(X, M^·(𝐔^u)) = U_n(X, M^·(𝐔))^{ū}, ū constant on the blocks of the function and equal to u at their centres, the first line of (3.38) read for a general u (p.278 l.3–12; the general form inferred, not printed).
- {#T11.9-0-3} Lemma 4's input space, continued. Conditions: O(1)Mα₁ ≤ β (p.278 l.46), (4B₃²O(1)M)²α₀ ≤ β (p.278 l.64), B₃²O(1)Mα₀ < ½α₁ (p.277 l.54). Lemma 4 as printed (p.280 l.24–32): for 𝐔 in that space, 𝐀 on □₀ with (3.31), and B′ on the bonds of the definition of U_j(□₀, ·) with abs(B′) < α₃, the pair (U_j, J_j)(□₀, exp i(τB + B′)) restricted to X lies in U^c_j(X, α₀, α₁), analytic, "for α₀, α₁, α₂, α₃ sufficiently small and satisfying all the restrictions".
- {#T11.9-0-4} Lemma 4's input space, continued. Here abs(𝐀₂), abs(∇^ξ𝐀₂) ≤ B₃ abs(B′) (p.280 l.4). B′ is the slot of the Cauchy integral (3.54) (p.280 l.35–42), over abs(σ) = r of 𝐄^{(j)} at exp i(τB + σB), ≤ E₀α₃^{−5}abs(B)⁵exp(−κd_j(X)); it is not RG I's step variable B′ = 𝒫B (#T10.13) and not the fluctuation.
- {#T11.10} CRE's class Ũ^c_j(X, α̃₀, α̃₁) (CRE p.261 l.5–40) is local on X. Its bounds are (2.34) on the plaquettes of U and 𝐔, (2.35) on the plaquettes of U_{p,X}(M^·(𝐔)), the configuration of the determining set 𝐁_p(X) ∪ {Γ_i}_{i<p} (#T6.11-1, #T11.27; l.10), (2.36) on 𝐉, (2.37) on 𝐉_{p,X}, (2.38) a gauge per cube, and (2.39) on A′.
- {#T11.10-0-1} CRE's class Ũ^c_j, continued. Each bound but (2.38) carries the factor 1 − β(1 − 2^{−(j−n)}) on X ∩ (Ω_n ∖ Ω_{n+1}). The boundary terms 𝐁^{(j)}(X, ·) of (2.41) are analytic on it with the bound (2.42), B₀e^{−κd_j(X)}, which has no age factor (p.261 l.52–66). Normalisations (p.261 l.10–22): (2.34) is α_{0,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2}, and (2.35) equals it; (2.36) is α_{0,n}(L^nξ)^{−3}, and (2.37) is #T6.11-1's.
- {#T11.10-0-2} CRE's class Ũ^c_j, continued. (2.39) is L^nξabs(A′), (L^nξ)²abs(∇_U^ξA′) < α_{1,n}. The class Ũ^c_j, the boundary terms' space (p.260 l.54–55), is invariant under G-valued gauges (p.261 l.42–43); no G^c-invariance is printed for it. The old terms' space U^c_j(X, α_{0,j}, α_{1,j}) of (ii) (no tilde, p.259 l.41–42; "These properties are the same as in [I.1]", p.259 l.44–45) is RG I's, a union of orbits of the G^c-valued transformations (I.1.10) (RG I p.262 l.4–14), so (iii) is G^c-invariance with no path condition. The old terms 𝐄^{(j)}(X, U_k, z) of CRE (2.27) (p.259 l.39–45): (i) depend on U_k restricted to X.
- {#T11.10-0-3} CRE's class Ũ^c_j, continued. (ii) extend to analytic functions of (𝐔, 𝐉) ∈ U^c_j(X, α_{0,j}, α_{1,j}), (I.1.9); (iii) the extension is invariant under the gauge transformations (I.1.10); (iv) it satisfies (I.1.18). With #T3.13's α_{·,n} (CRE (2.28)), CRE (2.6)–(2.7) (p.255 l.41–60) give α_{·,n} ≤ (1+β₀)²(n−m)^{1/2}α_{·,m} and α_{·,m} ≤ (1+β₀)²(n−m)^{β₀}α_{·,n} for n > m.
- {#T11.11} CRE Theorem 2 (CRE p.263 l.10–31). The age-decayed bound (2.43) is stated only for "sufficiently regular configurations U_k = U_k(V), e.g. for V restricted by the characteristic functions in (2.18)" (l.19–20), that is, at real backgrounds, and for it CRE puts 𝐀 = 0 in (I.3.30) (CRE p.280 l.18–19). Complex fluctuations enter through Lemma 4's 𝐀, the g^c-valued variable that replaces (tζ̃_□ + t_□ζ_□)𝐇_k(B′) in (I.3.28) (RG I p.276 l.27), not through its B′ (#T11.9-0-4).
- {#T11.12} CRE p.276 l.2–16 and (3.43) (p.276 l.26–32). Bałaban repeats Sect. 3 [I] on the multiscale cover: "the difference shows up in bounds only", η is replaced by L^{−n} for a cube of π_n, and the regularity of U_{k+1} near Ω_n ∖ Ω_{n+1} is "the same as for U_n-configurations", so "we obtain all the formulas and bounds of Sects. 3, 4 [I] with η replaced by L^{−n}".
- {#T11.12-1} The terms of the expansion (I.6.41) with localisation domains Y in Λ_{k+1} satisfy the conditions of Lemmas 5 and 6 [I] with the space (I.6.34). CRE's [I] is RG I (CRE p.285), which has §§0–5 only; its (6.x) and (7.x) are RG II's (1.x) and (2.x), as RG II prints "(6.6)" for its own (1.6) (p.3 l.16) and "(7.14)" for its own (2.14) (p.15 l.30). So (I.6.41) and (I.6.34) are RG II (1.41) and (1.34). Lemmas 5, 6 [I] are RG II's Lemmas 1 (p.9) and 2 (p.11), whose statements contain (1.34) and (1.41), identified by content, not printed (map #MD2.2.5.12-9).
- {#T11.12-2} The remaining terms, whose domains meet Λ_{k+1} without lying in it and which go into the boundary terms 𝐁^{(k+1)}, are analytic on (3.43), Ũ^c_{k+1}(Y, (1+β)α̃₀, (1+β)α̃₁) × {A : supp A ⊂ Y ∩ Λ^{(k)*}_{k+1}, abs(A) < C₁p₁(g_k)} (p.276 l.26–35), the analyticity space in the background 𝐔 and the fluctuation A of the localised interaction terms not contained in Λ_{k+1}. CRE writes p₁(·) only in (3.43) and defines it nowhere, nor do RG I, RG II, or UV3; it is not identified here with LFR I's p₁ (#T3.40).
- {#T11.12-2-1} No note uses (3.43)'s A-radius. The notes take these terms on O6's 𝒫_k^m (#T3.36; O6 §§3, 7; at k = 1 also the map, #MOMST.44-3–#MOMST.44-4, and the CA closure's b5 (1)), with the joint bound on 𝒫_k^{3/4} (#T4.50, O6 §5), which contains LF #4.595's A-ball abs(A) < 2r^χ_k at O6 §4's points (Cauchy #C8.5.1-7-1), and the Cauchy note bounds them by a_k on the cutoff ball (#C5.12-8, #C8.2.2).
- {#T11.12-3} CRE does not print that the points of his class lie on □₀ in Lemma 4's space U′^c(□₀, …), whose (iv) is on □₀'s maximal sequence while his class's (2.35) and (2.37) are on the configurations of the determining sets 𝐁_p(X) ∪ {Γ_i}_{i<p} (#T11.10); RG I p.275 l.19–21 does not either. The Cauchy note derives the inclusion at the points of 𝒮_z in (b) at radii at most r⁺, and uses it only there (#C5.12, #C5.22–#C5.34).
- {#T11.13} CRE p.280 l.14–20, the first case. It applies (3.3) and (3.18)–(3.28) [I], with U_k on Ω_n ∖ Ω_{n+1} a U_n-type configuration: "the difference is seen in bounds only". For the proof of Theorem 2 it puts 𝐇_k(B′) = 0 in (I.3.28), or 𝐀 = 0 in (I.3.30), and replaces L^{−1}η𝐇_{k+1} by η𝐇_k (l.18–19); (I.3.32) holds in the L^{−n}-scale (l.19–20).
- {#T11.13-0-1} CRE p.280, the first case, continued. RG I (3.18) (p.273 l.36–46): with □₀ = □̃⁵ and its sequence □₀ ⊃ ⋯ ⊃ □_{k+1} ⊃ □̃⁴, U_j(V) = U_j(□₀, M^·(U_j(V))), an identity without a gauge factor at the minimisers and the composites of their own data (g = 1); at LF's representatives W^u, c̃^u, and (c^□)^u, g = ū·u^{−1}, ū the block-constant transformation given by u at the block centres (VAR (181), p.307 l.7 and l.11–15), with Ad(g) within (1 + 2^{−9})² ≤ e^{2^{−8}} ≤ e^{β_I} of isometric, β_I := 2^{−7} (Cauchy #C5.26-13, LF #4.454, #4.467).
- {#T11.13-0-2} CRE p.280, the first case, continued. The fluctuation configuration (RG I p.265 l.33–41, p.266 l.18–21): with V = V′V^{(k)}, V^{(k)} = M^k(U_{k+1}) ((2.3)), and B′ = (1/i)log V′, U_k(V′V^{(k)}) = U′_k(V′)U_{k+1}, and the axial gauge and u_k give U′_k^{u_k^{−1}} = exp iη𝐇_k(B′), "regular and of the same order as B′".
- {#T11.13-0-3} CRE p.280, the first case, continued. The action is expanded at exp iη𝐇_k(B′)U_{k+1} by gauge invariance. RG I (3.28)–(3.31) (p.276 l.1–43): the old terms at the fluctuation configuration read U_j(□₀, M^·(exp iη(tζ̃_□ + t_□ζ_□)𝐇_k(B′) exp iL^{−1}η𝐇_{k+1})), the fluctuation part replaced by a 𝔤^c-valued 𝐀 with (3.31), abs(𝐀), abs(∇^η𝐀), ‖𝐀‖_{1,β} < α₂ on □₀, 0 ≤ β ≤ β₀ < 1, "satisfied by the function (tζ̃_□+t_□ζ_□)𝐇_k(B′) for ε₁ sufficiently small" (p.276 l.42), and 𝐇_{k+1} has such bounds with B₃O(1)Mα₀ on □̃⁴ (p.276 l.43).
- {#T11.13-0-4} CRE p.280, the first case, continued. CRE (3.15) (p.268 l.2–10) evaluates 𝐄_k + 𝐑_k + 𝐁_k at U_k(exp i[g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k)]V^{(k)}), so B′ = g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k) there; §8.2 reads it at the composite over 𝐔̂_z := U_{k+1}(□₀, M^·(𝐔)), 𝐔 a point of ℰ_{k+1}, with 𝐀_A = 𝐇_k(B′_A) (Cauchy #C8.2.2-18, #C8.2.2-19); at real minimisers 𝐔̂_z = U_{k+1}.
- {#T11.14} The Cauchy note's classes. The common polydisc 𝒫_j(X; s) (#C5.5) is the set of pairs whose G^c-orbit on X^∼ contains a representative (𝐔₀, 𝐉₀) satisfying (i)–(iii) of run A's U^c_j(X, s) with the current ≤ s_{0,j}/C_{Q*}, whose lift's G^c-orbit contains a configuration (ι_X𝐔₀)^ṽ with ‖Ad(ṽ^{±1})‖ ≤ e^{2β_I} satisfying (i), (ii), and the plaquette part of (iii) of run B's space (orbit-wise as RG I p.277 l.54–55; the gauges at the points used in Cauchy #C5.47).
- {#T11.14-0-1} The Cauchy note's classes, continued. The lifted pair then lies in run B's space at e^{2β_I}s, its current Q₁^{pl}(W_ṽ)*Ad(v)𝐉₀ bounded at the transformed pair (#C5.6), and e^{2β_I}·½α < α = α^B, run B's thresholds at the scale j being run A's (#C5.7, #CP.13-1); RG I's (iv) is not imposed and follows by (1.17) (RG I p.263 l.7–17) at 2B₃s ≤ ½α, so 𝒫_j is a union of G^c-orbits on which Δ is invariant (Cauchy §9.8).
- {#T11.14-0-2} The Cauchy note's classes, continued. The norm class is 𝒫_j(X; r) (#C5.8), with r = α′/2 = α/(8B₃) (#C5.4-3; #T3.13's α̃′).
- {#T11.14-0-2-0-1} The Cauchy note's classes, continued. 𝒞_k (#C5.9–#C5.11) is defined by (a), membership in the evaluation set ℰ_j(X; r) of the norm class on every X of the sum Δ(Λ_j, ·, z) (for (5.1c𝐀) on the near domains X ⊂ □̃² only, #C5.10–#C5.10-1), and (b), CRE (2.34), (2.36), (2.38), and (2.39) at the radii r on □₀ for an orbit representative 𝐔₀ = 𝐔^{h^{−1}} ((2.35) and (2.37) not imposed; CRE p.261 l.42–43 prints invariance under G-valued gauges only), RG I's analysis run at 𝐔₀ and transferred through c(𝐔, 𝐀) = c(𝐔₀, 𝐀^h)^{h̄}, 𝐀^h := Ad(h^{−1})𝐀 (Cauchy #C5.11, #C5.11-3, #C5.43-11).
- {#T11.14-0-3} The Cauchy note's classes, continued. Cauchy #C5.22–#C5.34 derive Lemma 4's input on 𝒮_z: (3.40) from (iii) through U_n(□₀, M^·(𝐔)) = 𝐔^g with ‖Ad(g)‖ ≤ e^{β_I} (#C5.26), and a □₀ meeting a finer layer read at the scale n − 1 with LM in place of M (#C5.33–#C5.35). The callers' complex points are the pairs (U_k(𝐕), J(U_k(𝐕))) at complex data 𝐕 that lie in Ũ^c_k(Y₄, α̃′/2), with RG II's s and the data segment's u entering only through the members' data (LF #4.454).
- {#T11.14-0-4} The Cauchy note's classes, continued. The age factor's configuration W (LF #4.454.11–#4.454.12) lies, at complex points, in 𝒞 for U_k at the callers' points of the kind (R1), those at the data on the margin ball sup_b abs(g_kCA(b)) ≤ m_k of Cauchy #C8.2.2, where Bałaban's representation exp iη𝐇(B′_A)U about each run's real minimiser (RG I p.265 l.37–41) puts both runs' own entries within 2^{−10} of the lift radius r/(2B₃) of the real ones (LF #4.521).
- {#T11.14-0-4-0-1} The Cauchy note's classes, continued. W lies in 𝒞⁺ for U^0_k at the other complex points and for U_k at the other callers' points (LF #4.454), whose lifts satisfy run B's conditions at (33/32 + o(1))r < r⁺ once r(Y) ≤ 1/64, not at r (LF #4.514-1, #4.521).
- {#T11.14-0-5} The Cauchy note's classes, continued. The representatives W^u of U″_k, U_{k,Λ}, and U_1 lie in (a⁺) and (b) at (65/64)r, their lifts at (17/16)r (LF #4.540), in the variant 𝒮⁺_z (Cauchy #C5.15) with the gauge u^{−1} (LF #4.536). U″_k := U_{𝐁″_k}(·) (LFR II (1.50), p.370 l.4) is (exp iη𝐇″_k(g_kB)U^0_k)^{u″_k^{−1}} ((1.51), p.370 l.11), with B as at #T12.9-1-0-5 and abs(log u″_k) ≤ 72d(max_mα_{1,m} + C_fac(N₀ + 1)^{1/2}max_mε_m) (LF #4.536-2-0.1, by BP_COMPLEX_BASE_GAUGE.md #4.536.10), and u_1's bound read at W_{X} := e^{iη𝐇_k(X)}𝐔 (LF #4.536-2-0); U_1 is the minimiser of (1.52)–(1.53) (p.370 l.18–33).
- {#T11.14-0-6} The Cauchy note's classes, continued. LFR II's (1.60) is printed at exp iη𝐇″_k(g_kB)U^0_k (p.372 l.24), and p.373 l.3–5 reads "using the representation (1.57), and then once more using the representation (1.58)". §8.2 uses the inclusion at the backgrounds 𝐔̂_z of the points of ℰ_{k+1} (Cauchy #C5.8, #C8.2.2), the fluctuation entering through the composites of (5.1c𝐀) on the ball of the margin m_k, not at general points of (3.43). 𝒮_z is defined at Cauchy #C5.13, the near-domain membership at #C5.21–#C5.34, and the norm d_j of §8.2 at #C8.2.2.
- {#T11.15} Bałaban's complex backgrounds and local functions. RG I p.273 l.39–46: for X ⊂ □̃², U_j(□₀) is built for □₀ = □̃⁵ "as in the condition (iv)", from a sequence □₀ ⊃ □₁ ⊃ ⋯ ⊃ □_{k+1} satisfying (1.3)–(1.6) [14], "hence □̃⁴ ⊂ □_{k+1}", so on □̃⁴ the data are those of the finest scale; (3.18) writes the configuration of (3.4) as U_j(□₀, M^·(U_j(exp iB_□Ū^j_{k+1}))). RG I p.275 l.2–7: in 𝐇_k and the averages Q_j of B̃_□, U_{k+1} is "replaced by the configuration U_{k+1}(□₀, M^·(𝐔)), extended as equal to 𝐔 outside □₀", which "has approximately the same regularity properties as 𝐔".
- {#T11.15-0-1} Bałaban's complex backgrounds and local functions, continued. The old term is then (3.24) 𝐄^{(j)}(X, U_j(□₀, exp iB̃_□M^·(U_{k+1}(□₀, M^·(𝐔))))) = (3.25) 𝐄^{(j)}(X, U_j(□₀, M^·(exp iη(tζ̃_□ + t_□ζ_□)𝐇_k(B′)U_{k+1}(□₀, M^·(𝐔))))), analytic on U′^c_{k+1}(□₀, (1+2β)α₀, (1+2β)α₁, α₀) (p.275 l.19–21). RG I (4.2)–(4.4) (p.281 l.21–41): 𝐄^{(j)}(X, U_j(□₀, exp iB)) = 𝐄^{(j)}(X, exp iξ𝐇_j(□₀, B)).
- {#T11.15-0-2} Bałaban's complex backgrounds and local functions, continued. Its B-derivatives at 0 are Cauchy integrals in τ_p of 𝐄^{(j)}(X, exp iξ𝐀), 𝐀 = Σ_pτ_p⟨δ^{n(p)}𝐇_j(□₀, 0), ⊗B_i⟩, the function (3.13) at 𝐔 = 1, 𝐉 = 0, analytic on (4.4) max{abs(𝐀)_X, abs(P₁(□₀)𝐀)_X, abs(∇^ξ𝐀)_X, abs(Δ^ξ𝐀)_X} < α₂. RG II p.5 l.2–3: in the term of Y₀ "all propagators and the function B are restricted to Y₀. This implies that this term depends on 𝐔, 𝐉 restricted to Y₀ ∪ □₀", the parameters s(Δ) being taken for Δ ⊂ Y₀ ∖ □̃⁴ (p.5 l.7, p.7 l.20).
- {#T11.16} Bałaban's determining sets and the 𝐑-step's large-field set. CRE (2.2) (p.255 l.15–16): Γ₀ = Ω₁^c, Γ_j = Ω_j^{(j)} ∖ Ω_{j+1}^{(j)} for 1 ≤ j ≤ k − 1, Γ_k = Ω_k^{(k)}, 𝐁 = ∪_jΓ_j.
- {#T11.16-0-1} Bałaban's determining sets and the 𝐑-step's large-field set, continued. So the top layer of the determining set is Ω_k. LFR II (1.33) (p.365 l.2–7): the new determining set after the 𝐑-operation is 𝐁_k = {Γ_j}, Γ_j = Γ″_j ∩ Z^c for j < k, Γ_k = Γ″_k ∪ Z^{(k)}; so all of the large-field domain Z, the components of both classes, goes onto the new top layer, and no bond moves to an older layer. (1.34): V on Z^c is V″, and V on Z is V′_kV_Λ. #T11.29's Λ, with k₀ = k − N₀ (LFR I p.181 l.40), lies in Ω^c_{k₀+1}, so it is at least N₀ layers old before R.
- {#T11.16-1} LFR II's new background field (LFR II (1.35), p.365 l.13–16). With V′_k = 1 on Λ^c and V_Λ defined on the whole of Z^{(k)}, equal to V_k outside Λ (l.13), "The new background field U_k is defined by U_k = U_{𝐁_k}(V)" (1.35), with 𝐁_k and V those of (1.33)–(1.34) (#T11.16).
- {#T11.17} The Cauchy note's evaluation set. §8.2 takes the norm d_j on ℰ_j(X; r) ⊂ 𝒫_j(X; r), the union of the G^c-orbits on X^∼ of the literal points (R1), (R2), (N), and (R3), on which Δ(X, ·) is constant (Cauchy #C5.8-1–#C5.8-21). The evaluations at its points are #C5.8-22–#C5.8-26, the inclusions of (N) and (R3) in 𝒫_j(X; r) for c₂ ≤ c_N are #C5.8-27–#C5.8-49, and the callers' points are #C5.8-50–#C5.8-52. LF's members at its other complex points are not literal points (#C5.8-12); the chains of members of members that bound them contract (LF #0.1.8-1, #0.1.12-1, #0.1.42-1).
- {#T11.18} VAR's R-gauge representative (VAR (145)–(153), p.301). U′_k satisfies (146) abs(Ū′_k^j − 1) < 9dL²Mε₀ − ε₀ on □̃^{(j)}. On ℭ_k = ∪_jΛ′_j ((148): Λ′₀ = □₁^c ∩ □̃, Λ′_j = □_j^{(j)} ∖ □_{j+1}^{(j)} for 1 ≤ j ≤ k − 2, Λ′_{k−1} = (□_{k−1}^{(k−1)} ∖ □_k^{(k−1)}) ∪ □′_k^{(k−1)}, Λ′_k = □″_k^{(k)}), V″ := Ū′_k^j on Λ′_j (149), so U′_k ∈ 𝔘_k({Ω′_j}, ε₀) ∩ 𝔙_k(ℭ_k, V″) ∩ Ax_k(ℭ_k, 1) (150), Ω′_j = □_j for j < k, Ω′_k = □″_k, and abs(V″ − 1) < 9dL²Mε₀ − ε₀ (151).
- {#T11.18-0-1} VAR's R-gauge representative, continued. Theorem 2 of his [6], applied to (U′_k, 1) under 9dL²Mε₀ ≤ c₁, gives a unique gauge u with ū^j = 1 on Λ′_j and U′_k^{u^{−1}} = U₁ = e^{iηA}, where L^jη abs(A), (L^jη)² abs(∇^ηA), (L^jη)³ abs(∂^{η*}∂^ηA), (L^jη)³ abs(Δ^ηA) < 9dL²B₁Mε₀ on Ω′_j for j = 0, …, k (152), and R∂^{η*}A = 0 (153), "where the operator R is defined for the sequence {Ω′_j}".
- {#T11.18-0-2} VAR's R-gauge representative, continued. These are size bounds with constants carrying L²B₁M, not linear-response bounds; R is the projection onto Δ_U N(Q′) (#T10.10). His [6] is REG (CMP 99, 75–102; VAR p.309), and ū^j = 1 is REG (1.29), (\overline{R₀u}^j)(y) = 1 for y ∈ Λ_j, the averaged gauge, whose linear part is Q′_j(U₀) (REG p.080 l.25–26; PBG p.393 l.46–p.394 l.2), not u(y) = 1.
- {#T11.18-0-3} VAR's R-gauge representative, continued. Hence U₁ is not in 𝔙_k (REG p.81 l.23–24: "The set Σ_k is not contained in 𝔙_k(𝔅_k, V) because the gauge transformations u do not satisfy the necessary conditions (1.14)"), its data obeying (1.30) instead. The preliminary gauge is the multiscale axial gauge REG (1.19) (p.81 l.6, #T11.33).
- {#T11.18-1} VAR Sect. F's hypotheses (VAR p.300 l.25–26). Sect. F proves "all the regularity properties of minimal configurations U_k. We will use only the fact that they are critical configurations of the functional (5) and that they belong to the spaces (6) with ε₀ sufficiently small." Its (145)–(153) are #T11.18.
- {#T11.19} VAR's spaces and invariance group (VAR (2)–(7), p.278). 𝔘_k({Ω_j}, ε₀) (from Sect. A of his [6]): abs(U(∂p) − 1) < ε₀L^{−2j} for p ∈ Ω_j, and abs((D_U^{η*}∂U)(b)) < ε₀L^{−2j}(L^jη)^{−1} for b ∈ Ω_j (2). 𝔙_k(𝔅_k, V): Ū^j = V on Λ_j, j = 0, …, k (3), V a fixed configuration on the multiscale lattice 𝔅_k. 𝔘_k is gauge invariant; 𝔙_k is invariant under the gauges with u(y) = 1 for y ∈ 𝔅_k (4), which form a group. The functional is A(U) = Σ_{p⊂Ω₀}η^{d−4}[1 − Re tr U(∂p)], η = L^{−k} (5), on 𝔘_k ∩ 𝔙_k (6), a union of orbits of that group.
- {#T11.19-0-1} VAR's spaces and invariance group, continued. For ε₀ small there is at most one critical orbit, and existence is proved under abs((∂V)(p′) − 1) < ε₁ for p′ ∈ 𝔅_k (7), a bond of p′ ∈ Λ_j with both ends in Λ_{j−1} carrying V̄ (p.278 l.40–44). By REG (1.14) (p.78 l.14–18), (Ū^u)^j_b = u(b₋)(Ū^j)_bu^{−1}(b₊) for b ⊂ T^{(j)}, so (4) preserves (3) only on bonds with both end-points at points of 𝔅_k. The data are these averages, the linearised residual group is N_𝔅 := {λ : λ = 0 at the points of 𝔅_k}, and Q̃D = D̄P (#T10.23).
- {#T11.19-0-2} VAR's spaces and invariance group, continued. This holds for RG I (0.4) (p.253) and for (0.12) (p.254), whose linear part is the same by (0.8) and (0.5) with U(x′, c₊) = U(c₊, x′)^{−1} (inferred, not printed). Their contour families 𝐆(c₋, x), 𝐆(c₊, x′) depend only on the block point and the site, so Q̃ = Q − D̄K, Q the line averages (PBG's, QD = D̄Q′) and KA(y) the average of A over the contours from y to the sites of B(y); for multipliers μ with D̄*μ = 0 the contour parts drop out of ⟨μ, Q̃t⟩ (LF #A.8).
- {#T11.19-0-3} VAR's spaces and invariance group, continued. A bond of T^{(j)} from Λ_j to a point of Ω_{j+1} off 𝔅_k, such as a unit bond from Λ₀ into Ω₁, is not a data bond, although #T11.19-2's bond convention counts it in Λ_j (inferred, not printed: the reading under which (4) preserves (3), as VAR p.278 l.16 and l.32 state). N_𝔅 is not PBG's N(Q′) = {Q′λ = 0}, which is the domain of PBG's gauge-fixing density (#T11.20) and the linearised group of REG (1.29) (#T11.18).
- {#T11.19-1} VAR's [6] is REG, "Spaces of regular gauge field configurations on a lattice and gauge fixing conditions", CMP 99, 75–102 (VAR p.309 l.26). REG Sect. A (p.76 l.28–47) defines (D^η_{U,μ}F)(x) := η^{−1}(R(U(x, x+ηe_μ))F(x+ηe_μ) − F(x)) and (D^{η*}_{U,μ}F)(x) := η^{−1}(R(U(x, x−ηe_μ))F(x−ηe_μ) − F(x)) (1.1), R(U)X := UXU^{−1} (l.40); for F on plaquettes, F_{μν}(x) := F(p_{μν}(x)), p_{μν}(x) := ⟨x, x+ηe_μ, x+ηe_μ+ηe_ν, x+ηe_ν⟩, μ < ν, and (D^{η*}_UF)(x, x+ηe_μ) := Σ_{ν<μ}(D^{η*}_{U,ν}F_{νμ})(x) − Σ_{ν>μ}(D^{η*}_{U,ν}F_{μν})(x) (1.2), the D_U^{η*} of VAR (2) and RG I (1.8).
- {#T11.19-2} A set Ω ⊂ T_η also denotes the bonds with at least one end-point in Ω, and the plaquettes likewise, on every lattice (REG p.77 l.16–19; VAR p.277 l.17, "at least one corner"). REG's 𝔘_k({Ω_j}, α₀) is a set of configurations "on T_η" (p.77 l.32) with "p ∈ Ω_j" in (1.7), "p ⊂ Ω_j" in (1.8), and b ∈ Ω_j in (1.9) (l.34–39), and REG admits Ω_j = T_η (l.29); VAR p.278 l.2 calls the same space one of configurations "on Ω₀", and VAR (5) sums over p ⊂ Ω₀. Neither paper prints how (1.9) is read at a bond of Ω₀ whose plaquettes reach bonds outside Ω₀ when Ω₀ ≠ T_η (both papers searched in full).
- {#T11.20} PBG's gauge fixing and G′ (PBG (3.17)–(3.24), pp.393–394; Theorem 3.1, p.397). The gauge-fixing density is exp(−‖D*A‖²/2α)(Z′^{−1}∫dλ δ(Q′λ)exp(−‖D*A^λ‖²/2α))^{−1} (3.17), A^λ = A − Dλ, with (Q′λ)(y) = (Q′_j(U)λ)(y) for y ∈ Λ_j (3.18), (Q′(V)λ)(y) = Σ_{x∈B(y)}L^{−d}R(V(Γ_{y,x}))λ(x) and Q′_j(U) = Q′(Ū^{j−1})⋯Q′(U) = Σ_{x∈B^j(y)}L^{−jd}R(U(Γ^{(j)}_{y,x}))λ(x) (3.19).
- {#T11.20-0-1} PBG's gauge fixing and G′, continued. "The above averaging operators Q′_j(U) are linear parts of the averaging operations \overline{Ru}^j for gauge transformations" (p.393 l.46–p.394 l.2; CMP98 (78)–(80), #T11.22). (3.17) = exp(−‖RD*A‖²/2α) (3.20), R = Δ_U^η N(Q′), N(Q′) = {λ : Q′λ = 0} (3.21), the orthogonal projection onto that subspace; Rf = Δ_U^ηλ₀ with λ₀ the minimum of ‖f − Δ_U^ηλ‖² over N(Q′) (3.22).
- {#T11.20-0-2} PBG's gauge fixing and G′, continued. Δ′_a = (Δ_U^η + Q′*aQ′) on Ω₀ (3.24), G′ its inverse, with Δ_U^η = D_U^{η*}D_U^η (3.23) (p.394 l.21), PBG's covariant Laplacian (#T10.13-2). Theorem 3.1: for M ≥ M₁ and U with (3.35) at Mα₀ ≤ a₀, abs(G′λ(x)), abs(∇G′λ(x)), abs(G′∇*λ(x)), abs(ΔG′λ(x)) ≤ B₀[(L^jη)², L^jη, L^jη, 1]e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}abs(λ) for x ∈ Δ(y), y ∈ Λ_j, supp λ ⊂ Δ(y′) (3.42), with M₁, δ₀, a₀, B₀ "dependent on d and L only". It bounds the scalar operator of R, not the response of the minimiser, which is VAR (190) (#T11.21).
- {#T11.20-1} PBG's covariant supremum norms (PBG (3.39), p.397 l.4–8). The norms are "identical to the norms used in [3, 4], e.g., given by (1.108), (1.109), but the derivatives there have to be replaced by the corresponding covariant derivatives determined by a configuration U": abs(A) = max_μ sup_x abs(A_μ(x)), abs(∇A) = max_{μ,ν} sup_x abs((D_μA_ν)(x)) (3.39).
- {#T11.20-2} PBG's penalty coefficients (PBG (3.24), p.394 l.24–29). ⟨λ, Q′*aQ′λ⟩ = Σ_{j=0}^ka_jΣ_{y∈Λ_j}(L^jη)^{d−2}abs((Q′_j(U)λ)(y))², where "the numbers a_j satisfy the recursive equations a_{j+1} = aa_j/(aL^{−2} + a_j), a_1 = a_0 = a > 0"; the recursion holds for j ≥ 1, inferred, not printed, since at j = 0 it would give a₁ = a/(1 + L^{−2}) ≠ a₀. Theorems 3.1–3.3 take a = 1 (p.397 l.38, p.398 l.60, p.399 l.18), and then 0 ≤ a_j − (1 − L^{−2}) ≤ L^{−2j} for j ≥ 1, so a_j ≤ 1 for all j (LF #4.101–#4.101-1). These a_j, written â_j at a = 1 in the Cauchy note (#C10.4.65-2), are not #T2.3's spacings a_j.
- {#T11.21} VAR (190) (p.308 l.38–48), the response of the representation field. abs(δ𝓗_μ(B, x)/δB_ν(y′)), abs(∇_x δ𝓗/δB_ν(y′)), ‖ζ∇δ𝓗/δB(y′)‖_β, abs(D*D δ𝓗/δB_ν(y′)), abs(Δ δ𝓗/δB_ν(y′)) ≤ O(1)[(L^jη)^{−1}, (L^jη)^{−2}, (‖ζ‖^ξ_β + abs(ζ))(L^jη)^{−2−β}, (L^jη)^{−3}, (L^jη)^{−3}](L^{j′}η)^{−d}exp(−δ₀d(y, y′)/8) for x ∈ Δ(y) or supp ζ ⊂ Δ̃(y), y ∈ Λ_j, y′ ∈ Λ_{j′}; from (189), (188), Lemma 2.1, Proposition 2, and (181).
- {#T11.21-0-1} VAR (190), continued. The print has δB_ν(y) without the prime in the fourth term, and the corrected file supplies it. 𝓗 is the field of the representation exp iη𝓗U_k with RD*𝓗 = 0 (VAR (21)); its derivative δ𝓗/δB has "regularity and decay properties identical to the propagator H, or H₀" (p.307 l.18). δ₀ is PBG's common decay rate of d and L (PBG p.423 l.39), and the O(1), like VAR's at p.299 l.26, depends on d and L only (inferred, not printed at (190)).
- {#T11.21-1} VAR's representation of the second variable (VAR (172)–(173), p.305 l.31–44). V = V′V₀, V₀ under (7), abs(V′ − 1) < C₁ε₁, "hence V′ = e^{iB′}, abs(B′) < 2C₁ε₁ on 𝔅_k" (172), "for ε₁ sufficiently small"; U₀ = U_k(V₀), and "We repeat the whole procedure of Sects. A–E for this pair" (l.31–37). In the Landau gauge U_k(V′V₀)U₀^{−1} = U₁ = exp iη𝓗(B), 𝓗 with (19)–(21) at ε₂ = B₁(B₃(1 + 4C₁)ε₁ + C₁ε₁) ≤ 6B₁B₃C₁ε₁ = B₅ε₁ (173) (l.39–43); "We assume that C₁ is an absolute constant, hence B₅ is such a constant also" (l.44).
- {#T11.21-2} 𝓗 = 𝒜₁ + H₁B − HD(𝒜₁ + H₁B) (174), 𝒜₁ + 𝔊((δ/δA′)V)(𝒜₁ + H₁B) = 0 (175), with 𝒜₁ under (19) at ε₂ = 8B₅ε₁ (VAR p.305 l.44–54). Proposition 9 (p.309 l.8–14): U_k(V′V₀) "in the axial gauge has an extension to an analytic function of G^c-valued small configurations V′ on 𝔅_k"; U_k(V′V₀)U_k(V₀)^{−1} in the Landau gauge is exp iη𝓗(B), B = (1/i)log V′, 𝓗 analytic in B and in the external U under (3.35)–(3.38) [5] or (1.7)–(1.9) [6], with (19)–(21) at ε₂ = B₅ε₁ and (190) (#T11.21). This B₅ is #T10.5's, not LFR I's B₅ of (1.78) (#T11.29-0); at C₁ = C_{V′} it is the LF's B₅^c (#T12.9-1).
- {#T11.22} The averaging of gauge transformations (CMP98 (78)–(81), p.30). (\overline{R₀v})(y) = v(y)exp[iΣ_{x∈B(y)}L^{−d}(1/i)log v^{−1}(y)R(V₀(Γ_{y,x}))v(x)] (78), defined inductively over the scales by (79)–(80), \overline{R₀u}^{j+1} = \overline{R(Ū₀^j)\overline{R₀u}^j}; the additional conditions (\overline{R₀u}^k)(y) = 1 for y ∈ Ω^{(k)} (81) fix the values of the axial gauge transformation at the coarse points. REG (1.29) imposes the same conditions at every layer (#T11.18). CMP98 is VAR's [4] and PBG's [5].
- {#T11.22-1} CMP98 Proposition 2 (CMP98 p.026 l.46–52). For U with (52), abs(U(∂p) − 1) < α₀η², η = L^{−k} (p.026 l.13–15), "with α₀ ≤ c₂ = min{1/(3C₀), ½c₂′}" (C₀ and c₂′ are #T4.56-0-1's C₀^{CMP98} and c₂′^{CMP98}, C₀ depending on d, c₂′ on d and L, p.026 l.8; this printed c₂ is not #T4.56-0-1's chain constant c₂^{CMP98}): abs(Ū^k(∂p) − 1) < α₀ + 2C₀α₀² < 2α₀ for p ⊂ Ω^{(k)} (54). "The result is local in the sense that if p = ⟨x, y, z, w⟩, then it is enough to assume (52) for p ⊂ B^k(x) ∪ B^k(y) ∪ B^k(z) ∪ B^k(w)" (l.52). VAR cites it as Proposition 2 [4] (p.278 l.46).
- {#T11.23} Lawler–Limic §6.5, "Capacity, transient case" (#T11.5, pp.136–139), for p ∈ 𝒫_d, d ≥ 3, A ⊂ ℤ^d finite. Es_A(x) = P^x{T_A = ∞}, cap(A) = Σ_{x∈A}Es_A(x), rad(A) = sup{abs(x) : x ∈ A} (p.136).
- {#T11.23-0-1} Lawler–Limic §6.5, continued. Proposition 6.5.1 (p.137): P^x{T_A < ∞} = C_d cap(A)/𝒥(x)^{d−2}·[1 + O(rad(A)/abs(x))] for abs(x) ≥ 2rad(A); the O-constant is not given. The proof's first part, for x ∉ 𝒞_{2rad(A)/δ} (δ with ℬ_n ⊂ 𝒞_{n/δ}), writes T̄_A and uses only the last-exit decomposition (Proposition 4.6.4) with G(x, y) = C_d𝒥(x)^{2−d} + O(abs(y)/abs(x)^{d−1}), a consequence of Theorem 4.3.1 (p.81; the proof names no theorem for it, so this source is inferred, not printed); there the constant depends on d and p only, uniformly in A.
- {#T11.23-0-2} Lawler–Limic §6.5, continued. The extension to 2rad(A) ≤ abs(x) by the Harnack inequality (Theorem 6.3.9) holds only for rad(A) at least that theorem's threshold N(K, U, p) and is not used. LF #0.1 takes abs(x) ≥ K₀rad(A), K₀(d) ≥ 3/δ making the O-term ≤ 1/2. Proposition 6.5.2 (p.137): printed cap(𝒞_n) = C_d^{−1}n^{d−2} + O(n^{d−1}), an error larger than the main term; the proof's last display, 1 = C_dn^{2−d}cap(𝒞_n)[1 + O(n^{−1})] (last-exit decomposition and Proposition 6.3.1), gives O(n^{d−3}), and LF #0.1 cites that display.
- {#T11.23-0-3} Lawler–Limic §6.5, continued. 𝒞_n = {𝒥 < n} is a discrete ball (𝒥*(x)² = d𝒥(x)² = x·Γ^{−1}x, (1.1), p.11, so 𝒥 = abs(·) for the simple random walk, Γ = d^{−1}I (p.82), and 𝒞_n = ℬ_n), not a cube; LF #0.1 applies 6.5.1–6.5.2 to a ball inside the box. Proposition 6.5.5 (p.139): cap(A) = sup Σ_{x∈A}f(x) over f ≥ 0 supported on A with Gf ≤ 1 on ℤ^d (monotonicity of cap; not used by LF). The "before leaving Q_{Kr}" form used in LF #0.1 (boundary Hölder exponent ν) is derived there from 6.5.1 by the strong Markov property; it is not printed.
- {#T11.25} VAR's problem at a base and PBG's linear problems (VAR Prop. 2, p.281; (41)–(49), pp.284–285; (74)–(84), pp.289–290; (99)–(104), p.293; PBG p.396 l.14–35, p.397 l.36–38, (3.109)–(3.110), p.417, (3.113)–(3.115), p.418, (3.127), p.421 l.1–20, (3.133), p.422). PBG's regularity class: cubes □ that are unions of big blocks of T_{L^{−j}} with □ ⊂ B^j(Λ_j) ∪ B^{j+1}(Λ_{j+1}) and □ ∩ B^j(Λ_j) ≠ ∅ (O(1) ≥ 10), Λ₀ = Ω₀ ∖ Ω₁ with Ω₀ any union of big blocks of T₁ containing Ω₁ (p.394 l.31–33), p.396 l.17 taking those at distance ≤ RM from Ω₁.
- {#T11.25-0-1} VAR's problem at a base and PBG's linear problems, continued. On each class cube there is a gauge with abs(A) < O(1)Mα₀(L^jη)^{−1}, abs(∇A) < O(1)Mα₀(L^jη)^{−2} (3.35), abs(∂*∂A) < O(1)Mα₀(L^jη)^{−3} (3.36), O(1)M the size of □; nothing is imposed outside Ω₀. BP_PBG_DRESSED.md, BP_PBG_COMPLEX.md, BP_PBG_3132.md, BP_REG_DRESSED.md (balaban_proofs/) and BETA's (P-PBG) (#B9.29-3) take Ω₀ = T, so Λ₀ = T∖Ω₁ (#BD.3a-0), as PBG p.408 l.21–22 does for the partition 𝒟.
- {#T11.25-0-2} VAR's problem at a base and PBG's linear problems, continued. PBG p.408 l.35–p.409 l.2 (every □̃⁵ in a class cube) needs Ω₀ = T, since for Ω₀ ≠ T a cube of 𝒟_0 meeting Ω₀^c lies in no class cube, every class cube lying in Ω₀; with Ω₀ = T it is proved with O(1) ≤ 12L by (Cl) (#BD.3a-1–#BD.3a-1a). Theorem 3.1 is #T11.20-0-2. HB minimises ½⟨A, Δ(U)A⟩ under L^jηQ_j(U)A = B on Λ_j and R(U)D_U^*A = 0 (3.109)–(3.110), L^jηQ_jA being the linear part of Q_j(U, ηA).
- {#T11.25-0-3} VAR's problem at a base and PBG's linear problems, continued. PBG's one-step average of U′ relative to U (CMP98's (89)–(92) with U for U₀, PBG p.392 l.45–46) is (\overline{R_{c−}U′})^{−1}(\overline{U′U})_c(Ū_c)^{−1}R̄_c\overline{R_{c+}U′} (p.418 l.5–7), with \overline{R_yU′} = exp[iΣ_{x∈B(y)}L^{−d}(1/i)log(R_yU′)(Γ_{y,x})] (3.113); its linear part gives Q_jDλ = D̄^jQ_j′λ (3.114)–(3.115), so QA is N(Q′)-invariant. H₁ is defined by (3.110) with the form ½⟨A, ΔA⟩ − ⟨HC^{(2)}(A), J⟩ (3.127), the form that replaces (3.109) once the non-linear averages of the later papers are linearised (PBG p.421 l.3–10).
- {#T11.25-0-4} VAR's problem at a base and PBG's linear problems, continued. (3.133) is a kernel bound of H with the decay O(1)(L^{j′}η)^{−d}e^{−½δ₁d(y,y′)}, and Theorem 3.12 (p.423) gives it for H₁. VAR: by Prop. 2 (p.281), every configuration of its axial space (18) is carried by the unique u with \overline{R₀u}^j = 1 (so u = 1 on Λ₀) to U₁U₀ with U₁ = e^{iηA}, (19), Q_j(U₀, ηA) = B with B given by REG (1.31) from V and U₀ (20), and R(U₀)D^*A = 0 (21); the map is one-to-one. VAR (44): Q_j(ηA) = L^jηQ_jA + C_j(L^jηA), abs(C_j) ≤ C₂(L^jη)²abs(A)², PBG's Q_j.
- {#T11.25-0-5} VAR's problem at a base and PBG's linear problems, continued. (47)–(49): A = A′ − HD(A′) makes the constraint linear, L^jηQ_jA′ = B (75), and D(A′) has no linear part ((78): its second-order part is C^{(2)}(A′)). (79): the quadratic part of the functional in A′ is ½⟨A′, Δ₁A′⟩ = ½⟨A′, Δ_πA′⟩ − ⟨HC^{(2)}(A′), J⟩, PBG's (3.127). (99)–(102): A′ = A₁ + H₁B, H₁ the operator of Δ₁ (VAR p.293 l.23–24), A₁ solving (101) with δA′ ∈ {QδA′ = 0, RD^*δA′ = 0} (100); (103)–(104) bound H₁B and A₁. VAR's data B relative to U₀ are REG (1.31)'s, not the RG I averages themselves.
- {#T11.25-1} VAR Proposition 3 (VAR p.289 l.30–34). The transformation (47) with (48), "linearizing the averaging operation Q(ηA), is defined and analytic for A′ satisfying (43) with ε₃ sufficiently small (e.g. 18C₂B₀dc₁(½)ε₃ ≤ 1, 2ε₃ ≤ c₄)"; "The range of this transformation contains the set (43) with ε₂ ≤ ¼ε₃, and is contained in the corresponding set with 2ε₃ instead of ε₂"; D(A′) satisfies (55) and its derivative (73). ε₃ is #T3.23's.
- {#T11.26} Bałaban's logarithm (CMP98 (21)–(25), p.21 l.26–50). log is "an inverse to the exponential function"; for abs(X − 1) < 1 it is the power series log X = Σ_{n≥1}((−1)^{n+1}/n)(X − 1)^n (21), and for a unitary U = Σ_je^{iλ_j}P_j, λ_j ∈ ]−π, π], log U = iΣ_jλ_jP_j = iA, A hermitian, abs(A) ≤ π (22)–(23). (24)–(25) print abs(U − 1) ≤ abs(log U) ≤ (π/2)abs(U − 1) (p.21 l.45–50). The later papers write log without a definition of their own (no other in the parts files).
- {#T11.26-1} For G ⊂ U(N) compact (V4 (H1)), log U ∈ i𝔤 for U ∈ G with abs(U − 1) ≤ δ_G, NG's constant of G (#T12.36-9), derived, not printed, at NG #NG8.1-0–#NG8.1-0.1 by the inverse function theorem for (A, B) ↦ e^{iA}e^{iB} on 𝔤 ⊕ 𝔤^⊥ and the closedness of G; used at LF #4.561.1-1-0.
- {#T11.27} CRE's localised determining sets (CRE p.256 l.55–63, p.257 l.1–25). For a domain Ω that "is a union of M₁-cubes in the lattice T_ξ, ξ = L^{−j}", Bałaban builds "a minimal determining set with a support in Ω, or a sequence of maximal domains Ω = Ω₀ ⊃ Ω₁ ⊃ ⋯ ⊃ Ω_j such that Ω_n is a union of L^nξM₁-cubes, and dist(Ω_n, Ω_{n−1}^c) ≥ L^nξM₁", the distance in T_ξ.
- {#T11.27-0-1} CRE's localised determining sets, continued. CRE adds that "dist(Ω_j, Ω^c) ≤ 2M₁, or Ω^{∼−2} ⊂ Ω_j", with ∼ taken for M₁-cubes. This set is 𝐁_j(Ω), and U(𝐁_j(Ω), ·) = U_{j,Ω}(·) (2.13). The joining (2.14): if ∂Ω ⊂ Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}, Ω is a union of M₁-cubes in T_ξ, and "the distance between ∂Ω and this boundary of Ω_j, Ω_{j+1}, which is contained in Ω, is greater than, or equal to 2M₁", then 𝐁 ∪ 𝐁_j(Ω) := (𝐁 ∩ Ω^{∼−2}) ∪ (𝐁_j(Ω) ∩ (Ω∖Ω^{∼−2})), and U(𝐁 ∪ 𝐁_j(Ω), ·) = U_{𝐁,Ω}(·) (2.15).
- {#T11.27-0-2} CRE's localised determining sets, continued. When ∂Ω lies in several Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}, the sets are built on each and (2.14)'s right-hand side is "summed up over the domains" (l.21–25). LFR I (1.19)–(1.20) (p.180 l.46–59) use this joining: 𝔹_k^{(n)}(Z) = 𝔹_k^{(n)} ∪ 𝔹_k(Z), and U^{(n)}_{k,Z}(V) = U(𝔹_k^{(n)}(Z), (M·(Q_k^{s*}V_k)⌈_{Z∩Ω_k}, V⌈_{Z∩Ω_k^c})). LFR II p.373 l.15–17: 𝐁_k″(Y₁) = 𝐁(Y₁) ∪ 𝐁_k″, for "the smallest localization domain Y₁ ∈ 𝐃_k containing X, and containing these components of Z, which intersect X".
- {#T11.27-0-3} CRE's localised determining sets, continued. This Y₁ need not be unique, since localization domains are wall-connected (RG I p.257 l.7–10): in d = 2 either of two cubes joins X to a component touching it at a vertex. For an old insertion F (BLL #BLL.3), the two-loops note takes Y₁ := D′(X(F)^{[2]}) = Y₁⁺(F) (#BLL.4-00, D′ of BP_TL_EVAL_POINTS #39-5-1), component by component, not BLL's Y₁(F) = D′(X(F)).
- {#T11.27-1} The unit of Bałaban's cube sizes (RG I p.257 l.2–4, l.11–12; LFR I p.178 l.51–52, p.179 l.7–8). In "the continuous space T scaled properly, so that it corresponds to the lattice T_ξ, ξ = L^{−j}", cubes "of a size M, where M = L^m" have "centers at points of the lattice T_M^{(j+m)}": lengths in T_{L^{−j}} are in units of j-blocks, an M-cube there is a (j+m)-block of side ML^jη in T_η, and the classes 𝐃_j "are connected by scaling transformations" (LFR I's "LM₂R_j-cubes of … T_{L^{−j}}, or the L^{−(k−j)}LM₂R_j-cubes of the lattice T_η" is this at η = L^{−k}).
- {#T11.27-2} So in #T11.27's (2.14) the M₁-cubes of T_ξ are M₁L^jη-cubes of T_η, ∼ adds one layer of them, and 2M₁ means 2M₁L^jη; in 𝐁_j(Ω), Ω_n is a union of M₁L^nη-cubes with dist(Ω_n, Ω_{n−1}^c) ≥ M₁L^nη, whatever j. CRE p.255 l.22–23's "(M₁η-cubes in the η-lattice)" is the case j = 0.
- {#T11.28} V on 𝐁, M_𝐁, and Q_k^{s*} (CRE p.256 l.26–53; (1.3), p.246 l.38–45; p.257 l.28–35). "In the first step the integration with respect to the variables U|_{Ω₁^c} = V₀ was left unchanged" (p.256 l.26).
- {#T11.28-0-1} V on 𝐁, M_𝐁, and Q_k^{s*}, continued. V = V_j on Γ_j, j = 0, …, k (2.10); M_𝐁(U) = M^j(U) on Γ_j (2.11); U_𝐁(V), also written U_k(V), is a minimal configuration of U → A^η(U) on {U regular, M_𝐁(U) = V} (2.12). (Q₁^{s*}V)(b) = 1 for b ⊂ B(y), y ∈ T^{(1)}, and V(c) for b ∈ B(c) = {b : b₋ ∈ B(c₋), b₊ ∈ B(c₊)}, c ∈ T^{(1)} (1.3). Q_k^{s*}V_k "is defined as in (1.3), only 1-blocks are replaced by k-blocks" (p.257 l.34), and U_{k,□}(V_k) = U(𝐁_k(□^{∼4}), M·(Q_k^{s*}V_k)) on the cubes □ of the LM₂R_k partition (2.16).
- {#T11.29} LFR I's Λ, U_{k,Z}, and χ_{k,Λ} (LFR I (1.73)–(1.75), p.192 l.40–55, p.193 l.1–28). Λ = (Ω^{∼4}_{k₀+1})^c ∩ Z (1.73), "the domain on which we integrate out all field variables". U_{k,Z}(V_k) = U(𝔹_k(Z), M·(Q_k^{s*}V_k)) (1.74), "defined in each component of Z separately".
- {#T11.29-0-1} LFR I's Λ, U_{k,Z}, and χ_{k,Λ}, continued. χ_{k,Λ} = χ({inf_{V_k⌈Λ} sup_{p∈Ω_k^c} abs(U_{k,Z}(∂p) − 1) < 2ε_kη²}) (1.75), a restriction "on the field V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c} only". The extension (l.10–20): a V_k on Z ∩ Λ^c with abs(V_k(∂p′) − 1) < ε, ε small, is extended through a generalized axial gauge on ∂⁺Λ to a configuration on the whole domain with abs(∂V_k − 1) < O(1)M²ε, and then abs(∂U_{k,Z} − 1) < O(1)B₃M²εη². Under χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4}) and χ_k″, V_k has an extension to Λ with abs(∂V_k − 1) < O(1)M²L²R_kε_k on Z, "hence the function U_{k,Z} is defined for all those fields" (l.21–25).
- {#T11.29-0} LFR I Proposition 1 (LFR I p.194 l.37–48). For V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c} with abs(∂V_k − 1) < ε on Z ∩ Λ^c, "for ε > 0 sufficiently small, there exists exactly one critical orbit of the function (1.77)"; an element is a minimum, V_Λ = V_Λ(V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c}), with abs(V_Λ(∂p′) − 1) < B₅M⁵ε for p′ ∈ Λ (1.78) (l.38–45). "The orbit-valued function V_Λ(V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c}) has an analytic extension for G^c-valued configurations 𝕍_k = V′_kV_k = exp iB′V_k satisfying the same regularity condition as V_k, with B′ ∈ 𝐠^c and small, e.g., abs(B′) < ε" (l.46–48). Λ is #T11.29's.
- {#T11.29-1} LFR I's representation of U_0 inside Λ (LFR I p.195 l.14–20, p.197 l.1–30). (1.80): abs(U_0(∂p) − 1) < 2ε_kη² + O(1)B₃B₅M⁵exp(−δ dist(p, Λ))ε_kη² for p ∈ Ω_k (p.195 l.15–19), which "follows also quite generally from the definition (1.79) and the bound (1.78)" (l.19–20). (1.83): V_Λ = M^k(U_0) satisfies (1.78) and, in the axial gauge in Λ^{(k)}, abs(V_Λ(b) − 1) < O(1)B₅M⁶ε_k for b ⊂ Λ^{(k)} (p.197 l.3–8).
- {#T11.29-2} U_0 is taken "in the axial gauge in k-blocks" inside Λ (p.197 l.9), and abs(M^j(U_0) − Q_{k−j}^{s*}V_Λ) < 11d²O(1)B₃B₅M⁵ε_k by (1.80) and (1.65) [14], which with (1.83) gives (1.84): abs(M^j(U_0) − 1) < O(1)B₃B₅M⁶ε_k inside Λ (l.9–14). (1.85): U_0 = U(𝔹_k(Λ), M^·(U_0)) inside Λ (l.16–19), 𝔹_k(Λ) as in #T11.27.
- {#T11.29-2-1} LFR I does not define the "axial gauge in k-blocks"; V_Λ = M^k(U_0) is in the axial gauge in Λ^{(k)} (p.197 l.3–4). The LF reads the gauge as REG (1.19) at the base 1 (#T11.33), inferred, not printed: LFR I p.197 l.10's (1.65) [14], 11d² and no α₁ term, is REG (1.65) (p.087 l.41–44) at REG's base U_{REG} := Q^{s*}_kV_Λ (LF #4.52-1-1.1), which is 1 inside the k-blocks (CRE (1.3), p.246 l.39–44, p.257 l.34).
- {#T11.29-2-1-1} So, inside Λ, the gauge transformations whose orbits it fixes have u = 1 at the corners y of the k-blocks (REG (1.12), (1.14), p.078 l.4, l.17; #T11.33-1), and the contour Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n} of PRT1 (1.7) (p.018 l.44–50), coordinate segments from x_{n+1} to x_n ∈ B(x_{n+1}), has at most d(L − 1) bonds of the level n, as 0 ≤ (x_n − x_{n+1})_μ ≤ (L − 1)L^nη by #T11.33-1's (2) (LF #4.53-1.0-1).
- {#T11.29-3} (1.86): expanding around the configuration 1 and transforming "to the Landau gauge constructed around this configuration", U_0 = (exp iη𝐇_{k,Λ}((1/i)log M^·(U_0)))^{u₀^{−1}}, and U_0^{u₀} = U_0^{(AL)} = exp iη𝔸₀ inside Λ (l.21–30). LFR I prints no normalisation of u₀; the LF (#4.561.1-1.3-0) reads it as VAR Proposition 2's at the base 1, the average of R₀u₀ at level j equal to 1 on Λ_j (VAR p.281 l.2), inferred, not printed.
- {#T11.30} LFR II's U_0 (LFR II (1.3)–(1.4), p.357 l.11–26, l.45–48). U_0 "is a minimum of the functional U → A(U), for U : U defined and regular on Z, M_{𝐁_k}(U) = M_{𝐁_k(Z)}(Q_k^{s*}V_k) on Z∖Λ" (1.3). By Sect. G [15], ⟨δA, J_0⟩ = 0 for all δA with Q_{𝐁_k(Z)}δA = 0 on Z∖Λ (1.4), J_0 the current and Q_{𝐁_k(Z)} the linear averaging defined by U_0 (l.24–25), "therefore J_0 = 0 on Λ" (l.26). In a proper gauge (Sect. F [15]), U_0 = exp iξA_0 on Z with abs(A_0), abs(∇^ηA_0) < O(1)M⁶R_kε_k (l.46–48).
- {#T11.30-0} LFR II's analytic extension in 𝐔 (p.358 l.24–30, l.39–43). The lower bound on (1.2)'s integral is needed "for the integral extended analytically on G^c-valued configurations 𝐔"; the extension replaces "the variables V_k in the function U_0" (#T11.30) "by the averages M^k(𝐔) for 𝐔 ∈ U^c_k(Z, α_{0,k}, α_{1,k})", the integrand is analytic in 𝐔, "and all the bounds above hold with ε_k replaced by O(α_{0,k} + α_{1,k}). This is an unessential change; it may result only in stronger restrictions on g_k, or γ" (l.26–30). The extended I(U_0) of (1.10) satisfies abs(I(U_0)) < O(1)M⁵ (1.11) (l.39–43).
- {#T11.30-0-1} LFR II's lower bound on the quadratic form (LFR II (1.7)–(1.9), p.358 l.1–23), after #T11.30's representation (p.357 l.45–48). "Using the bound (1.67) [10]", ⟨H_{1,k}B′, Δ₁(ζ₀)H_{1,k}B′⟩ ≥ γ₀‖∂B′‖² − O(1)(M⁶R_kε_k + exp(−R_k))‖B′‖² (1.7) (p.358 l.2–6), for the form in (1.2)'s exponent (p.357 l.4) at V′ = exp ig_kB′ (p.356 l.31), γ₀ LFR II's of (1.7) (#T3.14). For B′ on Λ, 0 on G₀, Σ_{b∈Λ}abs(B′(b))² ≤ d(100M)^{d+1}Σ_{p∈Λ}abs((∂B′)(p))² (1.8), since "Λ is a rectangular parallelepiped contained in a cube of the size 100M, and that G₀ determines the axial gauge in Λ" (l.7–13).
- {#T11.30-0-2} LFR II (1.9): ⟨H_{1,k}B′, Δ₁(ζ₀)H_{1,k}B′⟩ ≥ (γ₀/(2d(100M)⁵))‖B′‖² "for g_k sufficiently small. The above inequality holds for U_0 in an arbitrary gauge" (LFR II p.358 l.19–23). At d = 4, (1.8) gives ‖∂B′‖² ≥ ‖B′‖²/(d(100M)⁵), so (1.7) gives (1.9) once O(1)(M⁶R_kε_k + exp(−R_k)) ≤ γ₀/(2d(100M)⁵).
- {#T11.30-1} LFR II's extension of V_Λ and U_0 (LFR II (1.38)–(1.45), p.366 l.25–43, p.367 l.1–49). For 𝐔 ∈ U^c_k(Z, α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}), 𝐔 = U′U with U G-valued, abs(∂U − 1) < α_{0,k}η², and U′ = exp iηA′, abs(A′), abs(∇^η_UA′) < α_{1,k}, and M^k(𝐔) = M̃^k(U′)M^k(U) with M̃^k(U′) = exp iB̃′, abs(B̃′) < O(1)α_{1,k} (p.366 l.25–28). (1.38): V_Λ(M^k(𝐔)) = V′_Λ(M̃^k(U′))V_Λ(M^k(U)), "holding in the axial gauge", a definition of V′_Λ, whose arguments may be replaced by fields Ṽ′, V "satisfying proper smallness, or regularity conditions" (l.31–37).
- {#T11.30-1-0-1} LFR II's extension of V_Λ and U_0, continued. V′_Λ is a critical point of V′⌈_Λ → A(U_{k,Z}(Ṽ′V, V′V_Λ(V))) (1.39), V′ = exp iB′ with B′ "sufficiently small", expanded in (B̃′, B′), B̃′ = (1/i)log Ṽ′ (l.37–43).
- {#T11.30-2} LFR II (1.40) (p.367 l.2–10), the equation for the critical point B′ of (1.39) (#T11.30-1), has "exactly one solution B′_Λ(B̃′), which is an analytic function of the 𝐠^c-valued small field B̃′" (l.13–14), with abs(B′_Λ(B̃′)) ≤ 4d(100M)⁵γ₀^{−1}B₃²abs(B̃′) (1.41) (l.15–18), γ₀ LFR II's of (1.7) (#T3.14), and V′_Λ(M̃^k(U′)) = exp iB′_Λ((1/i)log M̃^k(U′)) (1.42) (l.19–23). (1.43): U_0(𝐔) = U_{k,Z}(M^k(𝐔), V_Λ(M^k(𝐔))) = U_{k,Z}(M̃^k(U′)M^k(U), V′_Λ(M̃^k(U′))V_Λ(M^k(U))) "in the axial gauge"; "Existence of the analytic extension follows from the results of Sect. G [15]" (l.25–30).
- {#T11.30-3} LFR II (1.44)–(1.45): M^j(U_0(𝐔)) = Ṽ_{j,Z}(M̃^k(U′))M^j(U_0(U)), Ṽ_{j,Z}(V′) analytic in (1/i)log V′ with abs(Ṽ_{j,Z}(V′) − 1) ≤ O(1)B₃B₅M⁵abs(log V′), "on the j-th domain of the determining set 𝐁_k(Z), for j = 0, 1, …, k. They all hold on the domain Λ" (l.31–46). On Λ, M^j(U_0(U)) satisfies LFR I (1.84) (#T11.29-2) "with α_{0,k} instead of ε_k", and 𝐇_{k,Λ} of LFR I (1.86) (#T11.29-3) has "the analytic extension 𝐀₀ = 𝐇_{k,Λ}((1/i)log M^·(U_0(𝐔))) satisfying (1.87) [IV], with α_{0,k} + α_{1,k} instead of ε_k" (l.46–49).
- {#T11.31} The localisation representations and their argument bounds. LFR II (1.16) (p.360 l.2–10): U_k″ = U″_{k,Z}(M·(U_k″)) = (exp iη𝐇″_{k,Z}((1/i)log[M·(U_k″)(M·(Q_k^{s*}V_k))^{−1}]⌈_{Z∖Z^{∼−1}})U″_{k,Z})^{u″^{−1}}. "The argument of the function 𝐇″_{k,Z} has a support in a layer of width 2M₁ at the boundary ∂Z, and it can be bounded by 44d²ε_k, as it follows from the restrictions given by the characteristic functions χ_k" (l.11–12).
- {#T11.31-0-1} The localisation representations and their argument bounds, continued. On the support of 1 − ζ₀, LFR II (1.36) (p.365 l.20–30), "a representation inverse to (1.16)": U″_{k,Z^{∼−3}} = U(𝐁_k″ ∪ 𝐁_k(Z^{∼−3}), [M·(Q_k^{s*}V_k)(M·(U_k))^{−1}]M·(U_k)) = (exp iη𝐇″_{k,Z^{∼−3}}(−(1/i)log[M·(U_k)(M·(Q_k^{s*}V_k))^{−1}])U_k)^{u_k^{−1}}, whose argument "has a support in the boundary layer of the width 2M₁ at the boundary ∂Z^{∼−3}, and it is bounded by 44d²ε_k".
- {#T11.31-0-2} The localisation representations and their argument bounds, continued. Expanding the Wilson action in (1.36)'s 𝐇-function leaves one term that is not small, −A((g_k(·))^{−2}(1 − ζ₀), U_k), g_k(·) the coupling function of p.365 l.19, which is combined with the Wilson action of (1.1)'s first exponential, transformed "with the configuration U_{k,Z} replaced by U_k, and with some new small terms" (l.30–36).
- {#T11.31-0-3} The localisation representations and their argument bounds, continued. CRE (1.21) (p.251 l.20–39): U₁ = U₁(Ω₁ ∩ Λ₁^c, M·(U₁)) = exp iL^{−1}𝐇_{1,Ax}(Ω₁ ∩ Λ₁^c, (1/i)log[M·(U₁)(M·(Q₁^{s*}V))^{−1}])U₁(Ω₁ ∩ Λ₁^c, V), where U₁(Ω₁ ∩ Λ₁^c, V) is determined as U₁(Ω₁, V) on (Λ₁^∼)^c and by 𝐁₁(Λ₁^c) and Q₁^{s*}V on Λ₁^∼∖Λ₁ (l.20–23); 𝐇_{1,Ax} depends on its argument restricted to "one layer of M₁-cubes touching ∂Λ₁ and contained in Λ₁^c", and on □′ ⊂ S₁, whose distance from the support of that argument is greater than MR₁, abs(𝐇_{1,Ax}) < O(1)B₃exp(−δMR₁)ε₁ (l.32–39).
- {#T11.32} The two-loops note's post-R map (BP_TL_EVAL_POINTS #39, #39-00, #39-1, #124, #133–#134; derived there, not printed). U^{post}(V) := U(𝐁_k″(Y₁), V) with 𝐁_k″(Y₁) of #T11.27; M is the averaging of 𝐁_k″(Y₁), finer than the post-R 𝐁_k on Z; P := U^{post} ∘ M (#39-00), not the P of other rows, and 𝓜 := {U^{post}(V) : V real}, the real post-R minimisers (#124), the real U with U = P(U) (#39-1), and 𝓜^c := {U^{post}(V) : V complex, within VAR's radius} its complexification, in which 𝓜 is totally real of maximal dimension (#124).
- {#T11.32-1} The two-loops note's post-R map, continued. On Y₁, U^{post}(M(U_{𝐁_k}(V))) = U_{𝐁_k}(V) up to a gauge transformation, by the nesting of 𝐁_k″(Y₁) over 𝐁_k and LFR II (1.61) (#133–#134).
- {#T11.33} REG's axial gauge relative to U₀, Ax_k(𝔅_k, U₀) (REG (1.16)–(1.20), p.078 l.40–49, p.079 l.4–15; CMP98 (64)–(69), p.29). With U = U′U₀ (1.16), U′ transforms by U′^u(x, x′) = u(x)U′(x, x′)R(U₀(x, x′))u^{−1}(x′) (1.17), R of #T11.19-1. For x₀ ∈ B^j(x_j), x_j ∈ Λ_j, 1 ≤ j ≤ k, and x_n ∈ B(x_{n+1}), (R̄^n_{0,x_{n+1}}Ũ′^n)(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}) = Π_{b⊂Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}}R(Ū₀^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},b₋}))Ũ′^n_b = 1 (1.19), with Ũ′^n = (\overline{U′U₀})^n(Ū₀^n)^{−1} (1.20, CMP98 (69)). It fixes one element in each orbit of the subgroup (1.14) (REG p.079 l.15).
- {#T11.33-0} For a general base U₀ it is not the absolute axial gauge (1.15), p.078 l.29–36; at U₀ = 1, Ū₀^n = 1 and R(1) is the identity, so (1.19) reads Ū′^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}) = 1, which is (1.15), REG's Ax_k(𝔅_k, 1) (p.097 l.43) (LF #4.52-1-1.1). The x_j are the corners of the blocks B^j(x_j) (CMP98 (2), #T11.33-1).
- {#T11.33-1} CMP98's blocks (CMP98 (1)–(4), p.017 l.17–31, p.018 l.2–7). Ω^{(j)} = Ω ∩ L^jηℤ^d (1), L > 1 a fixed integer; for y ∈ L^nηℤ^d "we define a block of an order j as the cube" B^j(y) = {x ∈ L^{−j}L^nηℤ^d : y_μ ≤ x_μ < y_μ + L^nη, μ = 1, …, d} (2), B := B¹, not the B of other rows; B^j(Λ) = ∪_{y∈Λ}B^j(y) (3); B^j(Ω^{(j)}) = Ω for j = 1, …, k, η = L^{−k}, "Thus Ω is a sum of blocks of the order k" (4).
- {#T11.33-2} Derived, not printed: for y ∈ L^jηℤ^d, B^j(y) is ηℤ^d ∩ Π_μ[y_μ, y_μ + L^jη). As L^kη = L^{k−j}·L^jη, each [y′_μ, y′_μ + L^kη), y′ ∈ L^kηℤ^d, is the disjoint union of L^{k−j} intervals [y′_μ + mL^jη, y′_μ + (m + 1)L^jη); so the order-j blocks partition each order-k block, and B^j(y) lies in exactly one B^k(y′), y′_μ = L^kη⌊y_μ/(L^kη)⌋.
- {#T11.33-2-1} By #T11.33-2's nesting, x_n ∈ B(x_{n+1}) iff B^n(x_n) ⊂ B^{n+1}(x_{n+1}), so a chain from x₀ has x₀ ∈ B^n(x_n), x_n the unique such point of L^nηℤ^d. So #T11.33's chain of a j-block is the first j links of the chain of the k-block containing it.
- {#T11.34} The chain's relative variables and gauge-transformation average (balaban_proofs/BP_REG_DRESSED.md #BR.2-1, #BR.2-2; CMP98 (78)–(80), p.030 l.15–31, with RG1's averaged contour variable W(y, x) of (0.11), p.253 l.43–47, for W(Γ_{y,x})). (R_{0,y}W′)(y, x) := (W′W₀)(y, x)W₀(y, x)^{−1} for W = W′W₀ bondwise, and (R_{0,y}v)(x) := R(W₀(y, x))v(x).
- {#T11.34-0-1} The chain's relative variables and gauge-transformation average, continued. The average (\overline{R₀v})(y) := v(y)exp[iΣ_{x∈B(y)}L^{−d}(1/i)log v^{−1}(y)R(V₀(y, x))v(x)] is #T11.22's (78) with V₀(y, x) for V₀(Γ_{y,x}), composed over the levels by (79)–(80). Its linear part at λ = (1/i)log v is Σ_{x∈B(y)}L^{−d}R(V₀(y, x))λ(x), so the composite's is #B7.14's Q′ (#T10.23).
- {#T11.35} The chain's axial gauge and norm (BP_REG_DRESSED.md #BR.2, #BR.2-3). Ax_k^{ch}(𝔅_k, U₀) is #T11.33's (1.19) with (R̄^n_{0,x_{n+1}}Ũ′^n)(x_{n+1}, x_n) = 1, in #T11.34's variables, in place of the product over Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}, on RG I's centred blocks, y ∈ B(y) (#T11.7-0-1), not #T11.33-1's corner blocks. In BP_REG_DRESSED, abs(·) is the operator norm in a fixed faithful unitary representation, on G and on 𝔤^c; it is Ad(G)-invariant, with κ_G^{−1}abs(X) ≤ abs(X)_BD ≤ κ_G·abs(X), κ_G ≥ 1 depending on G (#BR.2, #T4.56-1). REG's weighted norm is abs(A)_{(α)} := sup_jsup_{Ω_j}(L^jη)^{−α}abs(A) (REG p.086 l.19).
- {#T11.36} CMP98's classes of gauge transformations (CMP98 Sect. F, p.044 l.3–13, p.045 l.47–52, p.050 l.8–20, l.51–55). Λ_k(U₀, α₃): the u on Ω with abs((\overline{R₀u}^j)(x_j) − 1) < α₃ on Ω^{(j)}, j ≤ k (166), and abs((\overline{R₀u}^j)^{−1}(x_{j+1})(R̄^j_{0,x_{j+1}}\overline{R₀u}^j)(x_j) − 1) < α₃L^{j+1}η for x_j ∈ B(x_{j+1}), j < k (167).
- {#T11.36-0-1} CMP98's classes of gauge transformations, continued. The regular u′: abs(u′ − 1) < α₄ on Ω (176) and abs(u′^{−1}(b₋)R_{0,b}u′(b₊) − 1) < α₄η (177). For λ = (1/i)log u′, abs(D_{U₀}^ηλ), abs(λ) < α₄ (207) give (176)–(177) with 4α₄. For u₁ ∈ Λ_k(U₀, α₃) and u′ = e^{iλ}, λ 𝔤^c-valued, ũ′^j := \overline{R₀u′u₁}^j(\overline{R₀u₁}^j)^{−1} (178) (p.045 l.54–59), and Q′_j(u₁, λ) := (1/i)log ũ′^j (208) = Q′_jλ + C′_j(u₁, λ) (213), abs(C′_j) = O((α₃α₄ + α₄²)L^jη) (214).
- {#T11.36-0-2} CMP98's classes of gauge transformations, continued. (Q′_jλ)(y) is (212)'s first sum Σ_{x∈B^j(y)}L^{−jd}R(U₀(Γ^{(j)}_{y,x}))λ(x) (p.050 l.45; inferred, not printed, as CMP98 defines Q′_j nowhere else), with U₀(Γ^{(j)}_{x_j,x}) := Ū₀^{j−1}(Γ_{x_j,x_{j−1}})⋯Ū₀(Γ_{x₂,x₁})U₀(Γ_{x₁,x}) (p.029 l.66), so it is PBG's Q′_j(U₀) of (3.19) (#T11.20).
- {#T11.37} REG's statements used, recorded as statements (Bałaban's, accepted). Lemma 1 (p.079 l.38–49): under (1.7) at k = 1 and (1.24), abs(V′ − 1) < 4d²α₀ + α₁ on Ω₁ (1.25); its proof uses V′_b = 1 on the contour bonds (p.079 l.56). Proposition 3 (p.087 l.14). Theorem 4 (p.088 l.3), the form of Theorem 2 (p.083 l.17) that is proved, under (1.66) (p.087 l.48).
- {#T11.37-0-1} REG's statements used, continued. Proposition 5 (p.094 l.34–51): u′ = e^{iλ} with (1.107) and abs(λ), abs(Dλ)_{(−1)} < 8B₀′B₁(α₀ + α₁) (1.108), unique in (1.109). Proposition 7 (p.100 l.41). VAR (pp.277–309) cites REG throughout, among others its Lemma 1 (VAR p.303 l.16), (1.65) (p.301 l.6), Proposition 7 (p.299 l.28), Sect. D (p.297 l.9), Sect. F (p.278 l.50, pp.300–301), and Theorem 2 (p.280 l.51, p.301 l.50).
- {#T11.37-1} REG (1.36) and (1.47) (REG p.082 l.44–50, p.084 l.36–44; print lines, #T6.6). The gauge u with (1.29) is to make U₁ = U′^{u^{−1}} satisfy U₁ = e^{iηA}, abs(A) < B₁(α₀ + α₁)(L^jη)^{−1}, abs(∇^η_{U₀}A) < B₁(α₀ + α₁)(L^jη)^{−2}, ‖A‖_{1,β} < B₂(β₀)(α₀ + α₁)(L^jη)^{−2−β}, β ≤ β₀ < 1, on Ω_j, j = 0, 1, …, k (1.36). (1.47): (∂_{U₀}U₁)(p) − 1 = iη²(D^η_{U₀}A)(p) − ½η²V₂(U₀, A, ∂p) + O₁((1/3!)η³(∂abs(A)(p))³), "where V₂ is a polynomial of the second order in A". REG (1.1) is #T11.19-1.

- {#T12.27-1-2} m^X, the top index of 𝐁^X, the largest index of a point of 𝐁^X, with Ω^X_{m^X+1} := ∅ (#BC.3-1-0), m^X = 0 being trivial (#BC.4-6); a data bond of index n, an n-bond with an end-point of index n whose other end-point has index n or n − 1, or, at n = 0, lies outside Ω₀, the bonds and plaquettes of a set being those that intersect it (CRE p.255 l.20–21 with RG I p.251 l.26–28), that is, those with an end-point or a corner in it (VAR p.277 l.17; #BC.3-1-3–#BC.3-1-3-0), its datum Ū^n(c), inside Ω₀ REG's two cases of (1.30)–(1.31) (p.081 l.33–35), as in #T11.19 (#BC.3-1).
- {#T12.27-1-2-0} 𝔤^c := 𝔤 ⊗ ℂ = 𝔤 + i𝔤 ⊂ gl(N, ℂ), the sum direct as 𝔤 consists of Hermitian and i𝔤 of anti-Hermitian matrices (CMP98 p.020 l.30–31; LF #A.9.4-0.2, #4.561.1-1-0); BFWL writes g^c := g ⊗ ℂ (#B1.2), in either convention for g, and c_𝐠 for its constant in ‖ad X‖ ≤ c_𝐠abs(X), depending only on G (#B4.197-3, #B7.81-4), not #T4.51's c_g.
- {#T12.27-1-2-1} G^c := G·exp(𝔤), 𝔤 of Hermitian matrices, equal to the connected subgroup H of GL(N, ℂ) with Lie algebra 𝔤^c = i𝔤 ⊕ 𝔤, a real Lie algebra since [𝔤, 𝔤] ⊂ i𝔤, so that H is the subgroup generated by e^{𝔤^c} = e^{i𝔤^c} and contains G, which e^{i𝔤} generates (#BC.5-1; used in the LF at #4.563-2 and #4.563-3-0, among others).
- {#T12.27-1-2-1-0-1} G^c, continued. The polar map (u, X) ↦ ue^X is a diffeomorphism of U(N) × Herm(N) onto GL(N, ℂ), so on G × 𝔤 it is an injective immersion with closed image P := G·exp(𝔤), connected and through 1. Its tangent space at ue^X lies in ue^X𝔤^c, since Ad(e^{−X}) and (1 − e^{−ad X})/ad X map 𝔤^c into itself, and has the dimension 2dim 𝔤 = dim 𝔤^c. So P is a connected integral manifold through 1 of the left-invariant distribution 𝔤^c, open in its leaf H by the Frobenius theorem, and closed in H, H's topology being finer than GL(N, ℂ)'s; H being connected, P = H, a closed subgroup of GL(N, ℂ).
- {#T12.27-1-2-2} In CMP98, G = e^{i𝔤} is connected and G^c "may be defined as an image of the exponential function applied to" 𝔤^c, of which only a neighbourhood of G is used (p.020 l.30–31); that image need not contain a neighbourhood of G (in SL(2, ℂ) ⊃ SU(2), −(1 t; 0 1) is no exponential for any t ≠ 0, an exponential of trace −2 being −1), so the table reads CMP98's G^c as the generated subgroup (inferred, not printed).
- {#T12.27-1-3} BC's §G pair: r_B := C₁ε₁/2 ≤ log 2 and r_B^X := C₁ε₁^X/2 ≤ log 2, VAR's C₁ = L³ (p.280 l.20, #T4.6), so C₁ε₁, C₁ε₁^X ≤ 1 by ε₁, ε₁^X ≤ ε₁^G ≤ L^{−3}, and B₅ = 6B₁B₃C₁ = 30dL⁴B₀B₃ in (173), with #T3.32-1's B₁ = 5dLB₀ (#T11.21-1; #BC.5-1-0, #BC.5-1).

- {#T12.31} General-loops note (WILSON_IT1_GENERAL_LOOPS_2026_11_10.md, cited by #GL tag; derived, not reviewed), the loops. A loop C is a closed path of bonds of uL^{−q}ℤ⁴ for some q ≥ 0, u the fixed physical unit, ε = uL^{−K} (continuum #NWTOA.1), in any planes, visiting no site twice; its segments are its maximal straight subpaths, and its corners 𝒦(C), not #T12.13's 𝒦, the sites where consecutive segments meet, with perpendicular incoming and outgoing orientations (v_c, w_c) in 𝒱 := {±e_μ : μ ≤ 4}, not #T12.17's 𝒱(M) or #T12.25-1's 𝒱(X) (#GL1.1, #GL1.1-1).
- {#T12.31-0} A family 𝒞 = (C₁, …, C_{N_C}), N_C ≥ 1, consists of pairwise vertex-disjoint loops; N_C = 1 is the single-loop chain (#GL1.2). q(C) := the least q with every corner displacement in uL^{−q}ℤ⁴, and P^{co}(C) := the number of C's coarse bonds, of spacing uL^{−q(C)}, which is even (#GL1.1-2). The stopping scale is K′ = K − j, the general form of #T12.16's K′, with j the least j ≥ Q + q̄(j), Q := max(q(C_i), q_{ii′} : i ≠ i′), not #T10.23's operator Q, q̄ #T12.13's and q_{ii′} the least q with every displacement between corners of C_i and C_{i′} in uL^{−q}ℤ⁴ (#GL1.3, #GL6.1-4).
- {#T12.31-1} Lengths in step-k bonds, each its step-K′ value times L^{K′−k} and written with its argument (#GL1.4): P_k(C), the perimeter (the map's notation); s_k(C), the shortest segment, #T12.16's s_k for a square; δ_k(C), the least ℓ^∞-distance between non-adjacent segments, not #T3.27's cutoff δ_k; diam_k(C), the ℓ^∞-diameter. P_{K′}(C) = P^{co}(C)L^{j−q(C)} (#GL1.3-2); s_k(C), δ_k(C) ≥ max(MR_k, LMR_{k+1}), and ≥ L²MR_k for 1 ≤ k ≤ K′, with s_0(C), δ_0(C) ≥ L^{K′+3}M fine bonds (#GL1.4-2). For a square of side s_k, s_k(C) = δ_k(C) = diam_k(C) = s_k, P_k(C) = 4s_k, and abs(𝒦(C)) = 4 (#GL1.4-3).
- {#T12.31-1-1} diam_ph(C) := uL^{−K}diam_0(C) ∈ uL^{−q(C)}ℤ, the physical ℓ^∞-diameter of C in ℝ⁴. - {#T12.33} N-loops note (WILSON_IT1_N_LOOPS_UNIFORM_BOUND_2026_11_05.md, cited by tag), the separation devices. At a step k ≥ 1, δ := L²MR_k in step-k units, or L^{K′+3}M in fine units, is a lower bound on the ℓ^∞ distance between points of distinct loops (#NL1.3-2, #NL1.3-6); this δ is local to the note. N_i is the open ℓ^∞ δ/4-neighbourhood of C_i. An admissible piece is a closed connected set meeting at most one N_i, and for a finite family 𝒫 of them Σ(𝒫) := Σ_{p∈𝒫}diam_ℓ∞p (#NL1.3-6).
- {#T12.33-1} Tube hulls (#NL1.3-6.1). Let S′ be a connected union of a finite family 𝒫 of admissible pieces, and A ⊆ S′ a finite set of bonds of ⊔_iC_i. A cluster 𝒦 on C_i, local to this row and not a cluster of a polymer gas, is a component of the intersection graph of the pieces meeting N_i, and A_i(𝒦) is the set of the bonds of A on C_i in ∪𝒦. For A_i(𝒦) ≠ ∅, H_𝒦 is the smallest arc of C_i containing A_i(𝒦). These arcs are the tube hulls 𝒯(𝒫, A), with Σ_{H∈𝒯}abs(H) ≤ 4Σ(𝒫), and #𝒯 ≤ 4Σ(𝒫)/δ if A meets two loops (#NL1.3-6.2).
- {#T12.33-2} (H1′) (#NL6A.2-1): S_D is the union of a family 𝒫_D of pieces, each of diameter below δ/2 or within δ/2 of one loop at D's production step, with Σ(𝒫_D) ≤ u_DMσ(D) in production units, u_D being D's factor of two-loops' *Units with a factor* (#510, #566, #570; #NL6A.6); these pieces are admissible at every later step. For a C_i-insertion 𝒯(D) := {H(D)}, with H(D) the smallest arc of C_i containing ℐ(D), two-loops' arc set of D (#394, #574-1; #251 for a merged D, #518 for an 𝐑-made one). For a cross one 𝒯(D) := 𝒯(𝒫_D, A_D), with A_D := ℐ(D) ∪ (the spans of an 𝐑-made D, #NL6A.3). 𝒯(D) is fixed at production.
